ML Coding & System Design 面试准备

所有代码都实际跑过（numpy + PyTorch CPU），每个脚本末尾有 assert 自检，跑到 OK 就说明正确。

0. 面试形式

ML Coding

  • Google Colab，basic Python 环境
  • 不能用 AI coding assistant（练习时也要关掉 Colab 的 AI 补全）
  • 考 "foundational ML concepts"：从零手写 ML 组件，不是 LeetCode
  • 边写边讲：先说 shape 和思路，写完一段就跑一下

System Design

  • 按你的背景和你可能要做的工作出题
  • 共享屏幕，用 Excalidraw 画图
  • 中途可能在 Colab 里写一小段代码
  • 大概率是 research infra / agent 系统方向，而不是"设计 Twitter"
回 coordinator 时可以顺便问：每轮多长、面试官是谁。知道是谁就能查他的研究方向，大致猜到题目的侧重。

1. 准备计划

时间ML CodingSystem Design
Day 1MLP + softmax/CE + grad check（01）；LayerNorm/BatchNorm backward（02）画一张自己做过的系统的架构图，15 分钟能讲清楚（只讲 high-level）
Day 2Attention + causal mask / attention_mask（03）；GPT block + RoPE + KV cache + sampling（04）；MHA + KV cache + GQA（11）Mock：自我改进 / 自动化 ML 实验 agent 系统（题 A）
Day 3Optimizers（05）；Debug 题（06）；经典 ML（07）；LeetCode 模式 1–8（10）Mock：大规模 agent eval / rollout 平台（题 B）
Day 4RL losses：REINFORCE / GAE / PPO / GRPO（08）；LeetCode 模式 9–16（10）Mock：agent RL 训练 pipeline（题 C）+ 推理服务（题 D）
面试前一天不看答案，从空白 Colab 重写 03 + 04 + 11，计时 30 分钟写一遍 09 里的 worker pool / bandit / bootstrap；背 §4 的数字

练法：先看题目自己写，写完再对照 代码。每个脚本都有 assert 自检，python 0X_xxx.py 能跑到 OK 就算写对了。

2. ML Coding 题库

2.1 从零写模型组件（最常见）

题目面试官盯的点代码
numpy 手写 MLP forward + backward，gradient check 验证链式法则、每个矩阵的 shape、ReLU 的 backward、centered finite difference01
softmax + cross-entropy减掉每行最大值（log-sum-exp）；梯度 = (softmax − onehot)/N01
self-attention / multi-head attention除以 √d_head；mask 要在 softmax 之前加；causal mask 和 attention_mask 用 & 组合（True = 可以 attend）；(B,T,D)→(B,H,T,Dh) 的 reshape/transpose03
KV cache + GQA / MQA（高频，有公司要求一小时内连 MHA 一起写完）预分配 cache (B,Hkv,max_T,Dh) + 写入位置；prefill 和 decode；带 cache 时 causal mask 要对齐到末尾：tril(diagonal=S−T)；RoPE 用绝对位置，在写入 cache 之前作用到 k；GQA 把 q reshape 成 (B,Hkv,G,T,Dh)，k/v 广播，不用复制；repeat_interleave 也行，但会多存 G 份 K/V；cache 大小 = 2·L·Hkv·Dh·bytes/token，GQA 缩小 H/Hkv 倍11
Transformer blockpre-norm vs post-norm；残差；MLP 4× 扩展；SwiGLU04
位置编码：sinusoidal / RoPERoPE 是对 q、k 两两维度做旋转；只作用在 q、k 上，不作用在 v 上；使用 KV cache 时 position offset 要对04
LayerNorm / RMSNorm / BatchNorm forward + backward归一化的维度；BN 训练和推理行为不同（running stats）；LN backward 的紧凑公式02

MHA / GQA / MQA 对比

变体KV 头数KV cache 大小质量例子
MHAH1×最好GPT-2/3、Llama-1
GQAH/G（如 8）1/G接近 MHALlama-2-70B、Llama-3、Mistral
MQA11/H有损失PaLM、Falcon

为什么重要：decode 阶段受显存带宽限制，每步都要把整个 KV cache 读一遍，KV 越小吞吐越高、能放的 batch 和上下文越长。例：80 层、bf16、4k 上下文，MHA(64 头×128) 约 10 GiB/序列，GQA(8 头) 约 1.25 GiB。

2.2 训练相关

题目面试官盯的点代码
手写 SGD+momentum / Adam / AdamWbias correction；Adam 里的 L2 ≠ AdamW 的 decoupled weight decay（L2 会被 1/√v 缩放）05
完整训练循环（小 GPT 或 MLP，loss 要降）zero_grad 放在哪；model.train()/eval()；数据要 shuffle04
LR schedule：warmup + cosine为什么要 warmup（Adam 初期二阶矩估计不准，训练早期不稳定）04
gradient accumulation / clippingaccumulation 时 loss 要除以累积步数；clip 的是全局 norm04

2.3 推理和采样

题目面试官盯的点代码
greedy / temperature / top-k / top-ptop-p 要保留"累计概率刚好 ≥ p 的最小前缀"，边界别写错；temperature 不能为 0（要单独处理成 greedy）04
KV cache：先写 naive，再改成带 cacheprefill 和 decode 两个阶段；cache 的 shape (B,H,T,Dh)；带 cache 和不带 cache 的结果要完全一致；每生成一个 token 的复杂度从 O(T²) 降到 O(T)04
beam search（少见）length penalty；EOS 的处理—

2.3b Perplexity（评估类常考）

定义：PPL = exp(−(1/N) Σ log p(xi | x<i)) = exp(平均 token 交叉熵，单位 nats)。直觉：模型每一步平均在多少个等概率选项里"犹豫"。

题目面试官盯的点代码
给 logits 和 input_ids 算 PPL错位：logits[:, :-1] 预测 ids[:, 1:]；手写 log-softmax 要减 max；padding / prompt token 要 mask 掉；和 exp(F.cross_entropy(..., ignore_index=-100)) 对齐12
一个 batch / 整个语料的 PPL按 token 加权：exp(总 NLL / 总 token 数)。不是每条序列 PPL 的平均（短序列权重被放大，而且 mean(exp) ≥ exp(mean)）12
文本比 context 长sliding window：stride < window，窗口前段只当 context（label 设成 -100），每个 token 只算一次；stride = window（不重叠切块）更便宜，但每块开头的 token 上下文太少，PPL 偏高12
n-gram LM 的 PPL加 <s> / </s>；add-k 平滑让未见过的 n-gram 概率不为 0；OOV 映射到 <unk>；检查每个 history 下概率和为 112

常见追问：

  • sanity 值：完美模型 = 1；均匀分布 = V；刚初始化的 LM ≈ V（所以初始 loss ≈ ln V，vocab 50k 时约 10.8）
  • 以 2 为底：PPL = 2bits/token；bits/token = nats/token ÷ ln 2
  • 不同 tokenizer 的 PPL 不能直接比 → 用 bits-per-byte = 总 NLL / (ln 2 × 字节数)
  • PPL 低不等于生成好：不反映事实性、指令遵循、多样性；对 RLHF/对齐后的模型 PPL 通常会变高
  • 大语料累加 NLL 用 float64，避免精度损失

2.3c Online entropy / online softmax

题目：vocab 很大（比如 150k），logits 分块读进来，要求只扫一遍、不存整个概率分布，算出 softmax 的 entropy。思路和 FlashAttention 里的 online softmax 一样。

推导：pi = exp(xi) / Z，所以 H = −Σ pi log pi = −Σ pi(xi − log Z) = log Z − Ep[x]。每行只需要维护三个量：

  • m：目前见过的最大 logit（数值稳定用）
  • s = Σ exp(xi − m)：归一化项
  • t = Σ exp(xi − m) · xi：没归一化的 x 加权和

最后 log Z = m + log s，Ep[x] = t / s，所以 H = m + log s − t / s。每来一个新 chunk，如果最大值从 m 变成 mnew，旧的 s 和 t 都乘上 exp(m − mnew)（xi 本身不变，所以 t 和 s 用同一个缩放系数），再加上新 chunk 的贡献。

题目面试官盯的点代码
online logsumexp只维护 (m, s)；初始 m = −inf、s = 0；最大值变了要 rescale14
online entropy多维护一个 t；H = m + log s − t/s；和 -(softmax * log_softmax).sum(-1) 对齐；logits ≈ 1000 时也不溢出14
online cross entropyCE = logsumexp(x) − xtarget；target logit 在它所在的那个 chunk 里用 gather 取出来14
vocab 切到多张卡每张卡算自己那段的 (m, s, t)，合并时都 rescale 到两者中较大的 max 再相加；合并满足结合律，可以做 tree reduction / all-reduce14
样本流的经验 entropy维护 counts、N 和 S = Σ nc log nc；H = log N − S/N；每来一个样本只更新它那一项，O(1)14

常见追问：为什么要减 max（float32 下 exp(x) 在 x > 88 左右就溢出）；复杂度（时间 O(V)，每行额外内存 O(1)）；整段都被 mask 成 −inf 时 −inf − (−inf) = nan，要用 −1e9 之类的大负数代替；bf16 的 logits 也要用 float32 累加。

2.4 Debug 题

给一段训练代码，问 loss 为什么不降或者变成 NaN。常见埋的 bug：

  • 忘了 zero_grad()
  • F.cross_entropy 前面又做了一次 softmax
  • mask 方向反了
  • softmax / argmax 用错了维度
  • logits 和 labels flatten 的顺序不一致
  • 广播错误（(N,) 和 (N,1) 相减）
  • learning rate 太大 / 没有 warmup
  • 数据没有 shuffle
  • eval 时没调 model.eval() / 没加 no_grad
  • label 是 float 而不是 long
  • log(0) 导致 NaN
  • logging 时没 .item() 或 .detach()，导致内存泄漏

练习：06 里有一个埋了 6 个 bug 的版本，先自己找，再看修好的版本。

2.5 经典 ML（偶尔考）

logistic regression + GD、k-means（k-means++ 初始化，多跑几次初始化取最优）、PCA（用 SVD 实现）、kNN（向量化算距离）、conv2d / im2col。→ 07

2.6 RL（做 agent 的公司可能考）

题目要点
REINFORCE + baseline∇E[R] = E[∇log π · A]；baseline 能降方差，而且不引入 bias
GAEδt = rt + γV(st+1)(1−done) − V(st)；从后往前递推
PPO clipped objectiveratio = exp(logp_new − logp_old)；对 ratio·A 和 clip(ratio)·A 取 min；只在 response token 上算
GRPO同一个 prompt 采样 G 个回答，在组内标准化 reward 得到 advantage；不需要 value network
KL penaltyk3 估计器：exp(Δ) − Δ − 1，非负且无偏

→ 08

2.7 现场节奏

  1. 复述题目，确认输入输出的 shape 和边界条件（1 分钟）
  2. 口头讲思路，标出每个张量的 shape（2 分钟）
  3. 先写最简单、能跑通的版本，跑一下
  4. 加测试：拿 PyTorch 自带实现做参照，或者 gradient check，或者 sanity check（概率之和为 1、mask 掉的位置为 0）
  5. 再优化：数值稳定、向量化，讲清楚复杂度和显存
最常丢分的地方：shape 和广播出错、写不出数值稳定的版本、写完不验证。

2.8 LeetCode 风格算法题

Brandon 提到也可能出这类题。按"模式"准备比刷题量更有效：每个模式吃透一道代表题，碰到新题先判断它属于哪个模式。全部实现和测试在 10。

模式信号（看到这些就往这里想）代表题复杂度

ML 公司常考的采样、流式数据、稀疏计算Reservoir Sampling、Random Pick with Weight (528)、Sparse MatMul、Kth Largest (215, quickselect)—

现场流程

  1. 复述题目，问清楚输入范围、有没有重复、有没有负数、空输入怎么处理
  2. 先说暴力解法和它的复杂度，再说优化思路，面试官点头了再写
  3. 写的时候变量名要清楚，边写边说
  4. 写完手动走一遍小例子，再跑 edge case：空、单个元素、重复、全一样
  5. 最后主动说出时间和空间复杂度
要准备的题量：上面 16 个模式、约 30 道代表题，每道能在 15–20 分钟内写出来（不用 AI、不查文档）就够了。Python 常用工具要熟：collections.Counter / defaultdict / deque、heapq（默认是最小堆，最大堆要取负）、bisect、functools.lru_cache。

2.9 LLM 题 × LeetCode 格式（PyTorch）

别的候选人问过 recruiter：说的 "LeetCode style" 指的是形式，不是经典算法题。实际是给一个固定的函数签名，让你实现 ML 算子，比如 cross entropy，再用隐藏测试检查对不对。框架以 PyTorch 为主。

规则：只能用基础 tensor 操作，题目要你实现的东西不能直接调 F.xxx 或 nn.Xxx。测试会拿 torch 官方实现来对，还会专门测边界情况。

#题目隐藏测试最容易卡的地方
P01softmax / log_softmaxlogit = 1000 会溢出，先减 max；dim 不一定是 -1
P02cross_entropyignore_index（mean 的分母只算有效 token）；label_smoothing；三种 reduction
P03causal LM loss + PPLlogits[:, :-1] 对 ids[:, 1:]；padding 和 prompt 都要 mask（SFT 只算 response）
P04CE 手写 backwarddlogits = (softmax − onehot) / N
P05LayerNorm / RMSNorm方差用 biased；RMSNorm 不减均值、没有 bias
P06GELU / SiLU / SwiGLU FFNLlama FFN：W_down(silu(xW_gate) ⊙ xW_up)
P07Dropout除以 (1−p)；eval 原样返回
P08scaled dot-product attentionT≠S 时 causal 要 tril(diagonal=S−T)；整行被 mask 出 NaN → 0
P09RoPE / sinusoidal PERoPE 保持范数、内积只跟相对位置有关；offset 用绝对位置
P10Multi-head attentionpacked in_proj (3D, D) 按 q/k/v 拆；split heads → (B,H,T,Dh)，merge 时先 transpose 再 reshape；attention_mask (B,T,T) 加一个 head 维 broadcast，True = 可以 attend；和 nn.MultiheadAttention 对齐
P11GQA / MQAq 头 h 用 kv 头 h // G；和 SDPA(enable_gqa) 对齐
P12KV cache 逐 token decode结果必须等于 full forward；k 先加 RoPE 再进 cache
P13temperature / top-k / top-pT=0 = greedy；累计概率 ≥ p 的最小前缀；至少保留 1 个
P14AdamW 单步bias correction 的 t 从 1 开始；decoupled weight decay
P15全局 grad-norm clip + warmup-cosineclip 的是所有参数拼起来的 norm；返回 clip 前的 norm
P16KL：精确值 + k1/k3 估计器方向 KL(P‖Q)；k3 = r−1−log r 非负、无偏
P17序列 log-prob + DPO + Bradley-Terry只对 response token 求和；用 softplus(−z) 写，大 margin 不溢出；policy = ref 时 DPO loss = log 2
P18GRPO advantage + PPO clip组内 reward 全相同 → advantage 为 0；被 clip 的 token 没有梯度；只在 response token 上平均
P19MoE top-k router + 负载均衡 losstop-k 后重新归一化 gate；aux = E·Σ fiPi，均匀时 = 1
P20LoRAB 初始化为 0 → 一开始和 base 完全一样；scale = α/r；merge 后结果不变
P21BPE tokenizer按训练顺序 apply merge；encode→decode 能还原，包括中文等多字节字符

练习方式：先下载空白版 13_ml_leetcode_torch_blank.py（函数体都换成了 NotImplementedError，测试保持一样），自己填完跑到 OK，再对照答案 13。每题限时 10 到 15 分钟。

2.10 Streaming / 分块 / 省显存类题（Recursive 真题风格）

别人面 Recursive 时考过 online entropy。这类题的共同点：不允许把大的东西整个算出来（150k vocab 的完整 softmax、T×T 的 attention 矩阵、整个数据集、整个 batch），只能分块读、维护一个很小的 running state。关键是想清楚这个 state 怎么 rescale、两个 state 怎么 merge，最后结果要和 naive 版本完全一样。
#题目要维护的 state / 关键点测试对什么
S01online KL(P‖Q)，按 vocab 分块KL = EP[a−b] − logZa + logZb；P 侧像 online entropy 一样维护 (m, s, t)，Q 侧只需要 online logsumexpnaive KL；KL(P‖P)=0；logits ±1000
S02fused linear + cross entropy（不生成 N×V 的 logits）forward 按 vocab 分块算 hidden@Wcᵀ，同时做 online logsumexp、取出 target logit；backward 逐块重算 logits，dlogits = (softmax − onehot)/nvalid，再累加 grad_hidden 和 grad_W。Liger、Cut Cross-Entropy 用的就是这个思路loss 和两个梯度都和 autograd 一致，含 ignore_index
S03FlashAttention forward（分块 + online softmax）每个 query 行维护 (m, l, acc)；每来一个 key 块，l 和 acc 都乘 exp(mold−mnew) 再加上新块；causal 时跳过整块都在未来的 key 块；同时返回 lse = m + log l，给 backward 用和 SDPA 一致，测了 causal/非 causal、块大小不整除的情况
S04流式 mean / variance（Welford）+ 合并（Chan）维护 (N, mean, M2)；合并时 M2 = M2A + M2B + δ²·NANB/N；不要用 E[x²]−E[x]²，均值远大于标准差时会严重抵消。RL 里 reward 和 observation 的 normalizer 就是这么算的batch 大小不均匀、再合并后等于整体的 mean/var
S05分块 top-k；一遍完成的采样（Gumbel-max）top-k：维护当前最好的 k 个，和新块拼在一起再取 top-k；采样：argmax(x/T + Gumbel 噪声) 就是从 softmax 里的精确采样，argmax 可以跨块、跨卡直接合并，完全不用归一化和 torch.topk 一致；采样频率等于 softmax
S06Reservoir sampling（均匀 / 加权）第 i 个元素以 k/(i+1) 的概率替换一个随机位置；加权版给每个元素 key = log(u)/w，保留 key 最大的 k 个统计检验：每个元素被选中的概率是 k/n；加权时是 w/Σw
S07梯度累积时 loss 按 token 加权经典 bug：每个 micro-batch 先求 mean 再除以 micro-batch 数，有效 token 很少的 micro-batch 被放大。正确做法：先数所有 micro-batch 的有效 token 总数，每个 micro-batch 用 sum CE / 总数（多卡时 token 总数也要 all-reduce）累积的梯度等于整个 batch 的梯度，并且验证了错误写法对不上
S08Sequence packingposition_ids 在每条序列开头重置为 0；mask = 属于同一条序列 且 key ≤ query（block-diagonal causal）packed attention 等于每条序列单独算
S09Speculative decoding 的验证步骤以 min(1, p/q) 的概率接受；被拒时从 norm(max(p−q, 0)) 里重新采样，然后停止；K 个全部接受时再从 pK 多采一个 bonus token输出 token 的分布等于 target 的 p，而不是 draft 的 q
S10Causal linear attention：并行形式 vs 递推形式递推 state S = Σφ(k)ᵀv，z = Σφ(k)；每一步的显存不随 T 增长（KV cache 会随 T 增长）两种形式输出一致

练习方式：先下载空白版 15_streaming_ml_torch_blank.py（函数体换成了 NotImplementedError，测试不变），填完跑到 OK，再对照答案 15。online entropy 本身的推导和代码在 2.3c 和 14。

这类题常见的追问：state 为什么要 rescale，m 的初始值为什么是 −inf；两个 state 的合并满足结合律，所以可以做 tree reduction、跨卡 all-reduce；时间和显存复杂度；bf16 输入为什么要用 float32 累加；整块都被 mask 时怎么避免 NaN。

3. System Design

3.1 时间分配（按 45–60 分钟算）

  1. 澄清需求，5 分钟：目标是什么、规模多大（同时跑多少任务、算力预算多少）、怎么衡量成功、哪些地方允许出错
  2. 整体架构，10 分钟：先把各组件和数据流画出来，不要一开始就钻细节
  3. 深入 2–3 个组件，20 分钟：主动选你最强的部分，同时看面试官想往哪个方向追问
  4. 规模、失败和取舍，10 分钟：瓶颈在哪、挂了怎么恢复、成本怎么控制
  5. 怎么证明系统真的有效，5 分钟：research 公司特别看重这一块

3.2 题库

A. 自我改进 / 自动化 ML research 的 agent 系统（最可能）

给一个任务和评估指标，系统自己提方案、跑实验、迭代。

  • 生成候选：LLM 提方案，加上历史尝试记录（memory），避免重复试一样的想法；历史记录怎么压缩后喂给 LLM
  • 搜索策略：tree search、进化、bandit（UCB / Thompson sampling）、successive halving。讲清楚怎么平衡探索和利用，以及预算怎么分配
  • 执行：每个候选在沙箱里跑，用容器隔离，设好超时、CPU/GPU/内存限制和网络隔离
  • 评估：分开 held-out 和 validation 数据，用多个 seed 处理噪声，结果要做显著性检验（paired bootstrap）
  • 风险：过拟合 eval、reward hacking（改 eval 代码、读 test label、硬编码答案）、eval 泄漏。对策：eval 放在 agent 碰不到的环境里，把 validation 和 test 分开，加 sanity check，定期人工抽查
  • 工程：结果缓存（按代码 + 配置 + seed 做 hash）、重复候选去重、失败重试，每个改进怎么来的、跑出什么结果都要能追溯和复现
  • 指标：单位成本带来的提升、多久找到最优解、改进能不能迁移到 held-out 任务
B. 大规模 agent eval / rollout 平台
  • 任务队列 + 调度（K8s / Ray），GPU 推理和 CPU 沙箱分开调度和扩缩容
  • LLM 调用走一个单独的推理服务（vLLM / SGLang），做 batching 和限流；用外部 API 时要处理 rate limit、重试和成本上限
  • 沙箱：镜像预构建 + 缓存，冷启动要快；多步工具调用时环境状态怎么管理；超时处理
  • 结果的确定性：固定 seed / temperature，记录模型版本和镜像 hash
  • 存储：结构化结果放数据库，完整 trajectory 放对象存储；能按任务、模型、版本查询和对比
  • 可观测性：dashboard、失败原因自动分类（环境错误 / 超时 / 模型错误）、成本监控
  • 规模估算示例：1 万个 rollout × 每个 10 分钟 ÷ 2 小时完成 → 大约 830 个并发沙箱
C. Agent RL 训练 pipeline
  • actor（vLLM 生成 rollout）和 learner（FSDP / Megatron 训练）分开部署；同步训练 vs 异步训练
  • 异步训练时数据 staleness 怎么处理：限制 policy 版本差、用 importance ratio 修正或 clip、丢掉太旧的数据
  • 权重同步到 actor：NCCL broadcast / 写到共享存储，多久同步一次
  • 奖励：verifier / 单测 / reward model；长任务奖励稀疏，credit assignment 怎么做；防 reward hacking
  • 多轮工具调用：环境放在单独的服务里；tool 返回的 token 要 mask 掉，不算进 loss
  • 长尾问题：有些 rollout 特别长，会拖慢整批 → 部分 rollout 先训、超时截断、动态 batch
  • 监控：reward 曲线、KL、entropy、response 长度、各种 clip 被触发的比例
D. LLM 推理服务
  • continuous batching；prefill 和 decode 两个阶段的特点不同（prefill 受算力限制，decode 受显存带宽限制）
  • KV cache 显存管理：PagedAttention、prefix caching（相同的 system prompt / few-shot 前缀可以复用）
  • throughput 和 latency 的取舍：batch size、TTFT（首 token 延迟）、TPOT（每个 token 的生成时间）
  • 多模型路由、量化、speculative decoding、tensor parallel
E. 其他可能的题
  • 训练数据 pipeline：爬取 → 去重（MinHash）→ 质量过滤 → 配比；怎么防止 eval 数据污染训练数据（n-gram overlap 检测）
  • 大规模分布式训练：DP / TP / PP / FSDP 分别切什么；怎么存 checkpoint，节点挂了怎么恢复
  • 实验追踪和复现系统：怎么管理配置、代码版本（git commit）、随机种子、数据版本和产出文件
  • reward model / verifier 服务，以及怎么防 reward hacking

3.3 几乎每道题都会追问的点

  • 规模估算：多少张 GPU、跑多久、花多少钱、能撑多少并发
  • 出错了怎么办：任务挂掉、节点挂掉、结果不确定（同样输入跑出不同结果）、重试要幂等
  • 评估可信度：噪声怎么处理、要不要多个 seed、held-out 集、显著性检验
  • 安全和隔离：沙箱逃逸、网络访问、资源耗尽
  • 取舍：为什么选同步而不是异步，代价是什么，怎么缓解
  • 演进：先做 MVP，规模扩大 10 倍时哪里先成为瓶颈

3.4 中途可能要写的代码

一般是小组件，提前写一遍就不会卡（→ 09）：async worker pool 加超时、重试和指数退避；UCB bandit；paired bootstrap 显著性检验；结果缓存和去重；successive halving。

3.5 准备清单

  • ☐ 在 Excalidraw 里画一张自己做过的系统的架构图，15 分钟能讲完。只讲 high-level，不提客户名字和项目细节
  • ☐ A / B / C / D 每类题各做一次 mock，计时 45 分钟，大声讲出来，边讲边画
  • ☐ 练画图速度：真到面试时，大部分人卡在画图慢，而不是不会设计
  • ☐ 每个设计里都主动讲 trade-off："这里选 X，代价是 Y，所以加了 Z 来缓解"
  • ☐ 看一下对方公司的公开 blog / 论文和面试官的背景

4. 要背熟的数字

项目数
参数显存（bf16）2 bytes × 参数量：7B 约 14GB，70B 约 140GB
训练显存（mixed precision + Adam）约 16 bytes / 参数（bf16 权重 2、梯度 2、fp32 master 权重 4、Adam m 和 v 共 8），还没算 activation
KV cache 大小2 (K,V) × 层数 × KV 头数 × head_dim × 2 bytes，算出来是每个 token 的大小；Llama-2-7B（32 层，32 头 × 128）约 0.5MB/token，4k 上下文约 2GB；用 GQA 可以按比例缩小
训练 FLOPs≈ 6 × 参数量 × token 数（forward 2N + backward 4N）
推理 FLOPs≈ 2 × 参数量 / token
H100约 1000 TFLOPS（bf16 dense）、80GB HBM、带宽约 3.35 TB/s；实际 MFU 一般在 40–50%
decode 吞吐上限（batch 为 1）约等于显存带宽 ÷ 权重大小：7B bf16 在 H100 上约 3.35TB/s ÷ 14GB ≈ 240 tokens/s
Attention 复杂度时间 O(T²·d)，score 矩阵占显存 O(T²)（FlashAttention 不把整个矩阵放进显存）

01_mlp_numpy.py — MLP + softmax/CE + grad check (numpy) download · 
"""MLP from scratch in numpy: forward, backward, softmax-CE, gradient check, training.

Interview checklist:
- say shapes out loud: X (N, D), W1 (D, H), b1 (H,), W2 (H, C), b2 (C,)
- softmax must subtract the row max (log-sum-exp trick)
- dL/dlogits = (softmax - onehot) / N
- verify with a centered finite-difference gradient check
"""
import numpy as np


def softmax(z):
    """Row-wise softmax.

    Input:  z (N, C) raw scores (logits)
    Output: probs (N, C), every row is non-negative and sums to 1

    Formula: softmax(z)_j = exp(z_j) / sum_k exp(z_k)
    """
    # Step 1: subtract the max of each row. Softmax is shift-invariant, so the
    # result is unchanged, but now the largest exponent is exp(0) = 1 -> no overflow.
    row_max = z.max(axis=1, keepdims=True)              # (N, 1)
    z_shifted = z - row_max                             # (N, C)

    # Step 2: exponentiate.
    exp_z = np.exp(z_shifted)                           # (N, C)

    # Step 3: normalise each row so it sums to 1.
    row_sum = exp_z.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)          # (N, 1)
    probs = exp_z / row_sum                             # (N, C)
    return probs


def log_softmax(z):
    """Row-wise log-softmax, computed stably.

    Input:  z (N, C)
    Output: log_probs (N, C)

    Formula: log_softmax(z)_j = z_j - log(sum_k exp(z_k))
    Computing log(softmax(z)) directly can give log(0) = -inf, so we use the
    log-sum-exp form instead.
    """
    # Step 1: shift by the row max (same reason as in softmax).
    row_max = z.max(axis=1, keepdims=True)              # (N, 1)
    z_shifted = z - row_max                             # (N, C)

    # Step 2: log of the normaliser, log(sum_k exp(z_k)).
    sum_exp = np.exp(z_shifted).sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)  # (N, 1)
    log_normaliser = np.log(sum_exp)                    # (N, 1)

    # Step 3: subtract it.
    log_probs = z_shifted - log_normaliser              # (N, C)
    return log_probs


def cross_entropy(logits, y):
    """Mean cross-entropy loss.

    Input:  logits (N, C), y (N,) integer class labels in [0, C)
    Output: scalar loss = -(1/N) * sum_i log p(y_i | x_i)
    """
    N = logits.shape[0]
    log_probs = log_softmax(logits)                     # (N, C)

    # Pick out the log-probability of the correct class for each sample.
    row_indices = np.arange(N)                          # (N,)
    log_prob_correct = log_probs[row_indices, y]        # (N,)

    loss = -log_prob_correct.mean()                     # scalar
    return loss


class MLP:
    """Two-layer MLP: Linear -> ReLU -> Linear.

    Shapes:
        X  (N, D)  input batch
        W1 (D, H), b1 (H,)   first layer
        W2 (H, C), b2 (C,)   second layer
        logits (N, C)
    """

    def __init__(self, d_in, d_hidden, d_out, seed=0):
        """Create parameters.

        Weights use He initialisation (std = sqrt(2 / fan_in)), which keeps the
        activation variance roughly constant through ReLU layers.
        Biases start at zero.
        """
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

        std_W1 = np.sqrt(2.0 / d_in)
        W1 = rng.normal(0, std_W1, (d_in, d_hidden))    # (D, H)
        b1 = np.zeros(d_hidden)                         # (H,)

        std_W2 = np.sqrt(2.0 / d_hidden)
        W2 = rng.normal(0, std_W2, (d_hidden, d_out))   # (H, C)
        b2 = np.zeros(d_out)                            # (C,)

        self.params = {
            "W1": W1,
            "b1": b1,
            "W2": W2,
            "b2": b2,
        }

    def forward(self, X):
        """Forward pass.

        Input:  X (N, D)
        Output: logits (N, C)

        Also stores the intermediate values needed by the backward pass in self.cache.
        """
        W1 = self.params["W1"]
        b1 = self.params["b1"]
        W2 = self.params["W2"]
        b2 = self.params["b2"]

        # Step 1: first linear layer.
        h_pre = X @ W1 + b1                             # (N, H)

        # Step 2: ReLU non-linearity, max(0, x).
        h = np.maximum(h_pre, 0)                        # (N, H)

        # Step 3: second linear layer gives the class scores.
        logits = h @ W2 + b2                            # (N, C)

        # Save what backward needs: X for dW1, h_pre for the ReLU mask, h for dW2.
        self.cache = (X, h_pre, h)
        return logits

    def loss_and_grads(self, X, y):
        """Compute the loss and the gradient of the loss w.r.t. every parameter.

        Input:  X (N, D), y (N,) int labels
        Output: (loss scalar, grads dict with the same keys/shapes as self.params)

        Backward formulas (chain rule, from the loss back to the input):
            dlogits = (softmax(logits) - onehot(y)) / N        (N, C)
            dW2     = h^T @ dlogits                            (H, C)
            db2     = sum_over_batch(dlogits)                  (C,)
            dh      = dlogits @ W2^T                           (N, H)
            dh_pre  = dh * 1[h_pre > 0]                        (N, H)
            dW1     = X^T @ dh_pre                             (D, H)
            db1     = sum_over_batch(dh_pre)                   (H,)
        """
        # ---- forward ----
        logits = self.forward(X)                        # (N, C)
        loss = cross_entropy(logits, y)                 # scalar
        X, h_pre, h = self.cache
        N = X.shape[0]
        W2 = self.params["W2"]

        # ---- backward ----
        # Step 1: gradient of mean cross-entropy w.r.t. logits.
        # d(-log softmax_y)/dz = softmax(z) - onehot(y); the 1/N comes from the mean.
        probs = softmax(logits)                         # (N, C)
        dlogits = probs.copy()                          # (N, C)
        dlogits[np.arange(N), y] -= 1                   # subtract the one-hot target
        dlogits = dlogits / N                           # (N, C)

        # Step 2: second linear layer, logits = h @ W2 + b2.
        # dL/dW2 = h^T @ dL/dlogits
        dW2 = h.T @ dlogits                             # (H, C)
        # b2 is added to every row, so its gradient is the sum over rows.
        db2 = dlogits.sum(axis=0)                       # (C,)
        # dL/dh = dL/dlogits @ W2^T  (pass gradient to the previous layer)
        dh = dlogits @ W2.T                             # (N, H)

        # Step 3: ReLU, h = max(0, h_pre).
        # Derivative is 1 where h_pre > 0, else 0 -> gradient only flows through active units.
        relu_mask = (h_pre > 0)                         # (N, H) bool
        dh_pre = dh * relu_mask                         # (N, H)

        # Step 4: first linear layer, h_pre = X @ W1 + b1.
        dW1 = X.T @ dh_pre                              # (D, H)
        db1 = dh_pre.sum(axis=0)                        # (H,)

        grads = {
            "W1": dW1,
            "b1": db1,
            "W2": dW2,
            "b2": db2,
        }
        return loss, grads


def grad_check(model, X, y, eps=1e-5, n_samples=20, seed=0):
    """Compare analytic gradients with centered finite differences.

    For n_samples random coordinates of each parameter:
        numeric = (L(w + eps) - L(w - eps)) / (2 * eps)
        rel_err = |numeric - analytic| / (|numeric| + |analytic|)

    Returns the worst (max) relative error seen. Should be ~1e-7 or smaller in float64.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    _, analytic_grads = model.loss_and_grads(X, y)
    worst_rel_error = 0.0

    for name, W in model.params.items():
        for _ in range(n_samples):
            # Step 1: choose a random coordinate inside this parameter tensor.
            idx = tuple(rng.integers(0, dim_size) for dim_size in W.shape)
            original_value = W[idx]

            # Step 2: loss with the coordinate nudged up.
            W[idx] = original_value + eps
            loss_plus, _ = model.loss_and_grads(X, y)

            # Step 3: loss with the coordinate nudged down.
            W[idx] = original_value - eps
            loss_minus, _ = model.loss_and_grads(X, y)

            # Step 4: restore the parameter (very important!).
            W[idx] = original_value

            # Step 5: compare numeric slope with the analytic gradient.
            numeric_grad = (loss_plus - loss_minus) / (2 * eps)
            analytic_grad = analytic_grads[name][idx]
            denominator = max(1e-8, abs(numeric_grad) + abs(analytic_grad))
            rel_error = abs(numeric_grad - analytic_grad) / denominator

            worst_rel_error = max(worst_rel_error, rel_error)

    return worst_rel_error


def make_spirals(n=300, k=3, seed=0):
    """Toy dataset: k interleaved 2D spirals with n points each.

    Output: X (n*k, 2) float, y (n*k,) int labels in [0, k)
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    X_parts = []
    y_parts = []
    for class_id in range(k):
        radius = np.linspace(0.0, 1, n)                                 # (n,)
        angle_start = class_id * 4
        angle_end = (class_id + 1) * 4
        noise = rng.normal(0, 0.2, n)                                   # (n,)
        angle = np.linspace(angle_start, angle_end, n) + noise          # (n,)

        x_coord = radius * np.sin(angle)                                # (n,)
        y_coord = radius * np.cos(angle)                                # (n,)
        points = np.c_[x_coord, y_coord]                                # (n, 2)

        X_parts.append(points)
        y_parts.append(np.full(n, class_id))

    X = np.vstack(X_parts)                                              # (n*k, 2)
    y = np.concatenate(y_parts)                                         # (n*k,)
    return X, y


if __name__ == "__main__":
    X, y = make_spirals()
    model = MLP(2, 64, 3)
    # float64 + a random batch. Avoid points sitting exactly on the ReLU kink (e.g. X=0 with b1=0):
    # finite differences across the kink disagree with the analytic subgradient.
    idx = np.random.default_rng(1).choice(len(X), 16, replace=False)
    idx = idx[np.abs(X[idx]).sum(1) > 1e-3]
    err = grad_check(model, X[idx], y[idx])
    print(f"grad check max rel error: {err:.2e}")
    assert err < 1e-6

    lr = 1.0
    for step in range(2000):
        loss, grads = model.loss_and_grads(X, y)
        for k in model.params:
            model.params[k] -= lr * grads[k]
        if step % 500 == 0:
            print(f"step {step:4d} loss {loss:.4f}")
    acc = (model.forward(X).argmax(1) == y).mean()
    print(f"final loss {loss:.4f}, train acc {acc:.3f}")
    assert acc > 0.9
    print("OK")
02_norms_numpy.py — LayerNorm / RMSNorm / BatchNorm backward (numpy) download · 
"""LayerNorm / RMSNorm / BatchNorm forward + backward in numpy, checked numerically.

Interview checklist:
- LayerNorm normalises over the feature dim (per token); BatchNorm over the batch dim (per feature)
- BatchNorm keeps running stats for inference; LayerNorm behaves the same in train and eval
- LN backward compact form: dx = (1/sigma) * (g - mean(g) - xhat * mean(g * xhat)), g = dy * gamma
"""
import numpy as np


def layernorm_forward(x, gamma, beta, eps=1e-5):
    """LayerNorm forward: normalise each token over its last (feature) dim.

    Input:  x (..., D), gamma (D,), beta (D,)
    Output: y (..., D), cache for backward

    Formula:
        mu    = mean(x, last dim)
        var   = mean((x - mu)^2, last dim)
        xhat  = (x - mu) / sqrt(var + eps)
        y     = gamma * xhat + beta
    """
    # Step 1: per-token mean and variance over the feature dim.
    mu = x.mean(axis=-1, keepdims=True)                 # (..., 1)
    var = x.var(axis=-1, keepdims=True)                 # (..., 1)

    # Step 2: 1 / std. Kept separately because backward needs it.
    inv_std = 1.0 / np.sqrt(var + eps)                  # (..., 1)

    # Step 3: normalise -> zero mean, unit variance per token.
    x_centered = x - mu                                 # (..., D)
    xhat = x_centered * inv_std                         # (..., D)

    # Step 4: learnable scale and shift.
    y = xhat * gamma + beta                             # (..., D)

    cache = (xhat, inv_std, gamma)
    return y, cache


def layernorm_backward(dy, cache):
    """LayerNorm backward.

    Input:  dy (..., D) upstream gradient, cache from layernorm_forward
    Output: dx (..., D), dgamma (D,), dbeta (D,)

    Formulas:
        dbeta  = sum over all tokens of dy
        dgamma = sum over all tokens of dy * xhat
        g      = dy * gamma                                  (grad w.r.t. xhat)
        dx     = inv_std * (g - mean(g) - xhat * mean(g * xhat))   means over last dim
    The two mean terms come from mu and var both depending on every x_j in the token.
    """
    xhat, inv_std, gamma = cache
    D = xhat.shape[-1]

    # Step 1: parameter gradients. gamma/beta are shared by every token, so we sum
    # over all leading dims (flatten them into one "token" axis first).
    dy_flat = dy.reshape(-1, D)                         # (num_tokens, D)
    xhat_flat = xhat.reshape(-1, D)                     # (num_tokens, D)
    dgamma = (dy_flat * xhat_flat).sum(axis=0)          # (D,)
    dbeta = dy_flat.sum(axis=0)                         # (D,)

    # Step 2: gradient w.r.t. xhat (y = gamma * xhat + beta  ->  dL/dxhat = dy * gamma).
    g = dy * gamma                                      # (..., D)

    # Step 3: gradient through the normalisation.
    # - mean(g) term: removes the part of g that would just shift the mean (from d mu).
    # - xhat * mean(g * xhat) term: removes the part that would change the variance (from d var).
    g_mean = g.mean(axis=-1, keepdims=True)                     # (..., 1)
    g_dot_xhat_mean = (g * xhat).mean(axis=-1, keepdims=True)   # (..., 1)
    dx = inv_std * (g - g_mean - xhat * g_dot_xhat_mean)        # (..., D)

    return dx, dgamma, dbeta


def rmsnorm_forward(x, gamma, eps=1e-6):
    """RMSNorm forward: like LayerNorm but no mean subtraction and no beta.

    Input:  x (..., D), gamma (D,)
    Output: y (..., D), cache

    Formula:
        rms  = sqrt(mean(x^2, last dim) + eps)
        xhat = x / rms
        y    = gamma * xhat
    """
    # Step 1: root-mean-square over the feature dim.
    mean_square = (x * x).mean(axis=-1, keepdims=True)  # (..., 1)
    rms = np.sqrt(mean_square + eps)                    # (..., 1)

    # Step 2: rescale (no centering!).
    xhat = x / rms                                      # (..., D)

    # Step 3: learnable scale.
    y = xhat * gamma                                    # (..., D)

    cache = (xhat, rms, gamma)
    return y, cache


def rmsnorm_backward(dy, cache):
    """RMSNorm backward.

    Input:  dy (..., D), cache from rmsnorm_forward
    Output: dx (..., D), dgamma (D,)

    Formulas:
        dgamma = sum over all tokens of dy * xhat
        g      = dy * gamma
        dx     = (g - xhat * mean(g * xhat)) / rms
    Same as LayerNorm minus the mean(g) term, because there is no mean subtraction.
    """
    xhat, rms, gamma = cache
    D = xhat.shape[-1]

    # Step 1: gamma gradient, summed over every token.
    dy_flat = dy.reshape(-1, D)                         # (num_tokens, D)
    xhat_flat = xhat.reshape(-1, D)                     # (num_tokens, D)
    dgamma = (dy_flat * xhat_flat).sum(axis=0)          # (D,)

    # Step 2: gradient w.r.t. xhat.
    g = dy * gamma                                      # (..., D)

    # Step 3: through xhat = x / rms. rms depends on all x_j, which gives the
    # projection term xhat * mean(g * xhat).
    g_dot_xhat_mean = (g * xhat).mean(axis=-1, keepdims=True)   # (..., 1)
    dx = (g - xhat * g_dot_xhat_mean) / rms                     # (..., D)

    return dx, dgamma


class BatchNorm1d:
    """BatchNorm over a (N, D) batch: normalise each feature across the batch.

    Training: uses the batch mean/var and updates running estimates.
    Eval:     uses the running mean/var (so output doesn't depend on the batch).
    """

    def __init__(self, d, momentum=0.1, eps=1e-5):
        """d = number of features. momentum = weight of the new batch in the running average."""
        # Learnable scale and shift, one per feature.
        self.gamma = np.ones(d)                         # (D,)
        self.beta = np.zeros(d)                         # (D,)

        # Running statistics used at inference time.
        self.running_mean = np.zeros(d)                 # (D,)
        self.running_var = np.ones(d)                   # (D,)

        self.m = momentum
        self.eps = eps
        self.training = True

    def forward(self, x):
        """Forward pass.

        Input:  x (N, D)
        Output: y (N, D) = gamma * (x - mu) / sqrt(var + eps) + beta
        """
        if self.training:
            # Step 1a: statistics of THIS batch, per feature (reduce over axis 0 = batch).
            mu = x.mean(axis=0)                         # (D,)
            var = x.var(axis=0)                         # (D,)  biased (divide by N)
            n = x.shape[0]

            # Step 1b: update running averages for inference.
            # PyTorch stores the UNBIASED variance (divide by N-1) in running_var.
            unbiased_var = var * n / (n - 1)            # (D,)
            self.running_mean = (1 - self.m) * self.running_mean + self.m * mu
            self.running_var = (1 - self.m) * self.running_var + self.m * unbiased_var
        else:
            # Step 1 (eval): use the stored running statistics.
            mu = self.running_mean                      # (D,)
            var = self.running_var                      # (D,)

        # Step 2: normalise.
        inv_std = 1 / np.sqrt(var + self.eps)           # (D,)
        xhat = (x - mu) * inv_std                       # (N, D)

        # Step 3: scale and shift.
        y = xhat * self.gamma + self.beta               # (N, D)

        self.cache = (xhat, inv_std)
        return y

    def backward(self, dy):
        """Backward pass (training mode).

        Input:  dy (N, D)
        Output: dx (N, D); also sets self.dgamma (D,), self.dbeta (D,)

        Same compact formula as LayerNorm, but the means are over the BATCH axis (0):
            g  = dy * gamma
            dx = inv_std * (g - mean_N(g) - xhat * mean_N(g * xhat))
        """
        xhat, inv_std = self.cache

        # Step 1: parameter gradients (sum over the batch).
        self.dgamma = (dy * xhat).sum(axis=0)           # (D,)
        self.dbeta = dy.sum(axis=0)                     # (D,)

        # Step 2: gradient w.r.t. xhat.
        g = dy * self.gamma                             # (N, D)

        # Step 3: through the batch mean and batch variance.
        g_mean = g.mean(axis=0)                         # (D,)
        g_dot_xhat_mean = (g * xhat).mean(axis=0)       # (D,)
        dx = inv_std * (g - g_mean - xhat * g_dot_xhat_mean)    # (N, D)
        return dx


def numgrad(f, x, eps=1e-6):
    """Numerical gradient of a scalar function f() w.r.t. array x (modified in place).

    For every element: grad[i] = (f(x + eps e_i) - f(x - eps e_i)) / (2 eps)
    Output has the same shape as x.
    """
    grad = np.zeros_like(x)
    iterator = np.nditer(x, flags=["multi_index"])
    for _ in iterator:
        i = iterator.multi_index
        original_value = x[i]

        x[i] = original_value + eps
        f_plus = f()

        x[i] = original_value - eps
        f_minus = f()

        x[i] = original_value                           # restore
        grad[i] = (f_plus - f_minus) / (2 * eps)
    return grad


if __name__ == "__main__":
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    x = rng.normal(size=(4, 5, 8))
    gamma, beta = rng.normal(size=8), rng.normal(size=8)
    dy = rng.normal(size=x.shape)

    y, cache = layernorm_forward(x, gamma, beta)
    dx, dg, db = layernorm_backward(dy, cache)
    f = lambda: (layernorm_forward(x, gamma, beta)[0] * dy).sum()
    for name, a, n in [("dx", dx, numgrad(f, x)), ("dgamma", dg, numgrad(f, gamma)), ("dbeta", db, numgrad(f, beta))]:
        print(f"LayerNorm {name}: max abs err {np.abs(a - n).max():.2e}")
        assert np.allclose(a, n, atol=1e-6)

    y, cache = rmsnorm_forward(x, gamma)
    dx, dg = rmsnorm_backward(dy, cache)
    f = lambda: (rmsnorm_forward(x, gamma)[0] * dy).sum()
    assert np.allclose(dx, numgrad(f, x), atol=1e-6) and np.allclose(dg, numgrad(f, gamma), atol=1e-6)
    print("RMSNorm grads OK")

    bn = BatchNorm1d(8)
    xb, dyb = rng.normal(size=(16, 8)), rng.normal(size=(16, 8))
    bn.forward(xb); dx = bn.backward(dyb)
    f = lambda: (BatchNorm1d(8).forward(xb) * dyb).sum()
    assert np.allclose(dx, numgrad(f, xb), atol=1e-6)
    print("BatchNorm grads OK")
    print("OK")
03_attention_numpy.py — Multi-head attention + masks (numpy) download · 
"""Scaled dot-product + multi-head attention in numpy (causal mask + general attention_mask), checked against torch.

Interview checklist:
- scores = Q K^T / sqrt(d_head); mask BEFORE softmax with -inf (or a large negative number)
- attention_mask: bool (..., Tq, Tk), True = query i may attend to key j; one concept covers everything
- causal mask: position i may attend to j <= i  -> np.tril(ones) is the attention_mask
- shapes: x (B, T, D) -> q,k,v (B, H, T, Dh) with Dh = D // H
- complexity O(B * H * T^2 * Dh) time, O(B * H * T^2) memory for the score matrix
"""
import numpy as np


def softmax(z, axis=-1):
    """Numerically stable softmax along `axis`. Output has the same shape as z."""
    # Subtract the max so the largest exponent is exp(0) -> no overflow.
    z_max = z.max(axis=axis, keepdims=True)
    z_shifted = z - z_max
    exp_z = np.exp(z_shifted)
    exp_sum = exp_z.sum(axis=axis, keepdims=True)
    return exp_z / exp_sum


def scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, attention_mask=None):
    """Attention(Q, K, V) = softmax(Q K^T / sqrt(d), blocked entries -> -inf) V

    Input:
        q    (..., Tq, d)    queries
        k    (..., Tk, d)    keys
        v    (..., Tk, dv)   values
        attention_mask  optional bool, broadcastable to (..., Tq, Tk);
                        attention_mask[..., i, j] = True means query i may attend to key j
    Output:
        out  (..., Tq, dv)   weighted sum of values
        attn (..., Tq, Tk)   attention weights, each row sums to 1
    """
    d = q.shape[-1]

    # Step 1: similarity of every query with every key.
    k_transposed = np.swapaxes(k, -1, -2)               # (..., d, Tk)
    scores = q @ k_transposed                           # (..., Tq, Tk)

    # Step 2: scale by 1/sqrt(d). Without it the dot products grow like sqrt(d),
    # softmax saturates and gradients vanish.
    scores = scores / np.sqrt(d)                        # (..., Tq, Tk)

    # Step 3: apply attention_mask BEFORE softmax. Blocked pairs get a huge negative
    # score -> exp(...) = 0 -> zero weight. Each query row must allow at least one key.
    if attention_mask is not None:
        scores = np.where(attention_mask, scores, -1e30)    # (..., Tq, Tk)

    # Step 4: turn scores into probabilities over the keys (last axis).
    attn = softmax(scores, axis=-1)                     # (..., Tq, Tk)

    # Step 5: weighted average of the values.
    out = attn @ v                                      # (..., Tq, dv)
    return out, attn


def causal_mask(T):
    """Causal attention_mask: query i may only attend to keys j <= i (no peeking ahead).

    Output: (T, T) bool, True = query may attend to key. Just a special attention_mask;
    combine it with any other attention_mask using `&`.
    """
    ones = np.ones((T, T), dtype=bool)                  # (T, T)
    mask = np.tril(ones)                                # (T, T)
    return mask


class MultiHeadAttention:
    """Multi-head self-attention (no biases).

    x (B, T, D) -> project to Q, K, V (B, T, D) -> split into H heads (B, H, T, Dh)
    -> attention per head -> merge heads (B, T, D) -> output projection Wo.
    """

    def __init__(self, d_model, n_heads, seed=0):
        """Create the four (D, D) projection matrices Wq, Wk, Wv, Wo."""
        assert d_model % n_heads == 0, "d_model must be divisible by n_heads"
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        std = 1 / np.sqrt(d_model)

        # Drawn in this order: Wq, Wk, Wv, Wo.
        self.Wq = rng.normal(0, std, (d_model, d_model))    # (D, D)
        self.Wk = rng.normal(0, std, (d_model, d_model))    # (D, D)
        self.Wv = rng.normal(0, std, (d_model, d_model))    # (D, D)
        self.Wo = rng.normal(0, std, (d_model, d_model))    # (D, D)

        self.h = n_heads                     # number of heads H
        self.dh = d_model // n_heads         # per-head dim Dh

    def split(self, x):
        """Split the feature dim into heads: (B, T, D) -> (B, H, T, Dh)."""
        B, T, _ = x.shape
        x = x.reshape(B, T, self.h, self.dh)            # (B, T, H, Dh)
        x = x.transpose(0, 2, 1, 3)                     # (B, H, T, Dh)  heads become a batch dim
        return x

    def merge(self, x):
        """Inverse of split: (B, H, T, Dh) -> (B, T, D) by concatenating the heads."""
        B, H, T, Dh = x.shape
        x = x.transpose(0, 2, 1, 3)                     # (B, T, H, Dh)
        x = x.reshape(B, T, H * Dh)                     # (B, T, D)
        return x

    def __call__(self, x, attention_mask=None):
        """Forward pass.

        Input:  x (B, T, D)
                attention_mask optional bool, broadcastable to (B, H, T, T); True = query may attend to key
        Output: out (B, T, D), attn (B, H, T, T)
        """
        # Step 1: linear projections to queries, keys, values.
        q_full = x @ self.Wq                            # (B, T, D)
        k_full = x @ self.Wk                            # (B, T, D)
        v_full = x @ self.Wv                            # (B, T, D)

        # Step 2: split into H heads, each of size Dh.
        q = self.split(q_full)                          # (B, H, T, Dh)
        k = self.split(k_full)                          # (B, H, T, Dh)
        v = self.split(v_full)                          # (B, H, T, Dh)

        # Step 3: attention inside each head independently.
        head_out, attn = scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, attention_mask)   # (B, H, T, Dh), (B, H, T, T)

        # Step 4: concatenate heads back together.
        merged = self.merge(head_out)                   # (B, T, D)

        # Step 5: output projection mixes information across heads.
        out = merged @ self.Wo                          # (B, T, D)
        return out, attn


if __name__ == "__main__":
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    B, T, D, H = 2, 6, 16, 4
    x = rng.normal(size=(B, T, D))
    mha = MultiHeadAttention(D, H)
    # attention_mask: (B, 1, T, T), True = query i may attend to key j (broadcast over heads).
    # Batch 0: plain causal. Batch 1: causal AND keys 4, 5 are blocked for every query.
    attention_mask = np.zeros((B, 1, T, T), dtype=bool)
    attention_mask[0, 0] = causal_mask(T)
    attention_mask[1, 0] = causal_mask(T)
    attention_mask[1, 0, :, 4:] = False
    out, attn = mha(x, attention_mask)
    print("out", out.shape, "attn", attn.shape)

    # sanity: causal -> upper triangle of attention is zero; rows sum to 1
    assert np.allclose(np.triu(attn[0, 0], 1), 0)
    assert np.allclose(attn.sum(-1), 1)
    # sanity: blocked keys (positions 4,5 of batch 1) get zero weight
    assert np.allclose(attn[1, :, :, 4:], 0)

    try:
        import torch
        import torch.nn.functional as F
        t = lambda a: torch.tensor(a, dtype=torch.float64)
        q, k, v = (t(mha.split(x @ W)) for W in (mha.Wq, mha.Wk, mha.Wv))
        ref = F.scaled_dot_product_attention(q, k, v, attn_mask=torch.tensor(attention_mask))  # torch bool mask: True = may attend
        ref = t(mha.merge(ref.numpy())) @ t(mha.Wo)
        print("max diff vs torch:", (ref - t(out)).abs().max().item())
        assert torch.allclose(ref, t(out), atol=1e-8)
    except ImportError:
        print("torch not installed, skipped reference check")
    print("OK")
04_gpt_torch.py — Mini GPT: RoPE, KV cache, sampling, AdamW, warmup/cosine (torch) download · 
"""Minimal GPT in PyTorch: RoPE, RMSNorm, causal MHA with KV cache, SwiGLU MLP, AdamW + warmup/cosine,
grad clipping, grad accumulation, and sampling (temperature / top-k / top-p).

Trains a char-level model on a tiny repeated text and checks:
  1) loss goes down
  2) KV-cache generation == full-recompute generation (greedy)

Interview checklist:
- pre-norm residual: x = x + attn(norm(x)); x = x + mlp(norm(x))
- weight tying between token embedding and lm_head
- AdamW: decoupled weight decay; common choice: decay only >=2D weights (matmuls, embeddings), not norms/biases
- KV cache: per layer store k,v of shape (B, H, T_past, Dh); new token attends to all of them; no mask needed for 1 query
- per-token cost with cache: O(T * D) attention instead of O(T^2 * D)
"""
import math
import torch
import math
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

torch.manual_seed(0)


# =====================================================================================================
# RMSNorm
# =====================================================================================================
class RMSNorm(nn.Module):
    """Root-mean-square layer norm (used by Llama instead of LayerNorm).

    Unlike LayerNorm it does NOT subtract the mean and has no bias; it only rescales each vector so that its
    root-mean-square is 1, then multiplies by a learned per-channel gain.

        y = x / sqrt(mean(x^2) + eps) * w

    Input:  x (..., D)
    Output: y (..., D)   same shape
    """

    def __init__(self, d, eps=1e-6):
        super().__init__()
        self.w = nn.Parameter(torch.ones(d))   # learned gain, starts at 1 so the norm is initially "pure"
        self.eps = eps                         # avoids division by zero for an all-zero vector

    def forward(self, x):
        # Step 1: mean of squares over the feature dimension
        mean_square = x.pow(2).mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)             # (..., 1)

        # Step 2: 1 / RMS  (rsqrt = 1 / sqrt, one fused op)
        inv_rms = torch.rsqrt(mean_square + self.eps)                 # (..., 1)

        # Step 3: normalise, then apply the learned per-channel gain
        x_normed = x * inv_rms                                        # (..., D)
        return x_normed * self.w                                      # (..., D)


# =====================================================================================================
# Rotary position embedding (RoPE)
# =====================================================================================================
def rope_cache(T, dh, base=10000.0):
    """Pre-compute the cos / sin tables used by RoPE.

    RoPE treats each head vector as dh/2 pairs of numbers (x0,x1), (x2,x3), ... and rotates pair i by the angle
    position * theta_i, where theta_i = base^(-2i/dh). Low i -> fast rotation (fine position info),
    high i -> slow rotation (coarse / long-range position info).

    Input:  T   max sequence length, dh head dimension (must be even)
    Output: cos, sin  each (T, dh/2); entry [t, i] = cos/sin(t * theta_i)
    """
    # Step 1: one frequency per pair of dimensions
    pair_index_times_2 = torch.arange(0, dh, 2).float()                # (dh/2,)  = 0, 2, 4, ...
    inv_freq = 1.0 / (base ** (pair_index_times_2 / dh))               # (dh/2,)  theta_i

    # Step 2: angle for every (position, frequency) combination
    positions = torch.arange(T).float()                                # (T,)
    angles = torch.outer(positions, inv_freq)                          # (T, dh/2)

    return angles.cos(), angles.sin()


def apply_rope(x, cos, sin):
    """Rotate every consecutive pair (x0,x1), (x2,x3), ... of the last dimension by its position's angle.

    2D rotation of a pair (a, b) by angle phi:
        a' = a * cos(phi) - b * sin(phi)
        b' = a * sin(phi) + b * cos(phi)

    Input:  x (B, H, T, dh);  cos, sin (T, dh/2) -- already sliced to the T positions of x
    Output: (B, H, T, dh) rotated, same shape as x
    """
    # Step 1: split into the "first" and "second" element of every pair
    x_even = x[..., 0::2]                                              # (B, H, T, dh/2)  a's
    x_odd = x[..., 1::2]                                               # (B, H, T, dh/2)  b's

    # Step 2: apply the 2D rotation to every pair (cos/sin broadcast over B and H)
    rotated_even = x_even * cos - x_odd * sin                          # (B, H, T, dh/2)
    rotated_odd = x_even * sin + x_odd * cos                           # (B, H, T, dh/2)

    # Step 3: interleave them back into the original layout (a0', b0', a1', b1', ...)
    stacked = torch.stack([rotated_even, rotated_odd], dim=-1)         # (B, H, T, dh/2, 2)
    return stacked.flatten(-2)                                         # (B, H, T, dh)


# =====================================================================================================
# Causal multi-head self-attention with optional KV cache
# =====================================================================================================
class Attention(nn.Module):
    """Causal multi-head self-attention with RoPE and an optional KV cache.

    Input:  x (B, T, D); cos, sin (T, Dh/2) for the absolute positions of these T tokens;
            cache: None (no caching) or a dict that this layer reads/writes keys "k", "v" of shape (B, H, T_past, Dh)
    Output: (B, T, D)
    """

    def __init__(self, d, h):
        super().__init__()
        self.h = h              # number of heads
        self.dh = d // h        # dimension per head
        # One fused matmul produces q, k and v at once (3 * d outputs) -- faster than three separate Linears.
        self.qkv = nn.Linear(d, 3 * d, bias=False)
        # Output projection that mixes the heads back together.
        self.o = nn.Linear(d, d, bias=False)

    def forward(self, x, cos, sin, cache=None):
        B, T, D = x.shape

        # Step 1: project to q, k, v and split into heads.
        qkv = self.qkv(x)                                              # (B, T, 3*D)
        qkv = qkv.view(B, T, 3, self.h, self.dh)                       # (B, T, 3, H, Dh)
        qkv = qkv.permute(2, 0, 3, 1, 4)                               # (3, B, H, T, Dh)
        q = qkv[0]                                                     # (B, H, T, Dh)
        k = qkv[1]                                                     # (B, H, T, Dh)
        v = qkv[2]                                                     # (B, H, T, Dh)

        # Step 2: add position information with RoPE (only q and k; v carries content, not position).
        # Done BEFORE caching, so the cached keys already carry their absolute position and never need re-rotating.
        q = apply_rope(q, cos, sin)                                    # (B, H, T, Dh)
        k = apply_rope(k, cos, sin)                                    # (B, H, T, Dh)

        # Step 3: KV cache. Append the new keys/values to the ones from earlier steps, then store the result.
        if cache is not None:
            if "k" in cache:
                k = torch.cat([cache["k"], k], dim=2)                  # (B, H, T_past + T, Dh)
                v = torch.cat([cache["v"], v], dim=2)                  # (B, H, T_past + T, Dh)
            cache["k"] = k
            cache["v"] = v
        Tk = k.shape[2]   # total number of keys we attend over (= T without a cache)

        # Step 4: attention scores, scaled by 1/sqrt(Dh) so the softmax doesn't saturate as Dh grows.
        scores = q @ k.transpose(-1, -2)                               # (B, H, T, Tk)
        scores = scores / math.sqrt(self.dh)                           # (B, H, T, Tk)

        # Step 5: attention_mask -- a bool tensor (T_q, T_k), True = this query may attend to this key.
        # Here it is just the causal mask: the T queries are the LAST T positions of the Tk keys, so query i
        # (absolute position Tk - T + i) may see keys 0 .. Tk - T + i  ->  tril with diagonal offset Tk - T.
        # Without a cache Tk == T and this is the usual lower triangle; with 1 decode query it is all True.
        attention_mask = torch.ones(T, Tk, dtype=torch.bool, device=x.device)
        attention_mask = attention_mask.tril(diagonal=Tk - T)          # (T, Tk)
        # Where the mask is False, set the score to -inf so softmax gives that key weight 0.
        scores = scores.masked_fill(~attention_mask, float("-inf"))    # (B, H, T, Tk)

        # Step 6: softmax over keys -> attention weights, then weighted sum of the values.
        weights = scores.softmax(dim=-1)                               # (B, H, T, Tk)
        per_head_out = weights @ v                                     # (B, H, T, Dh)

        # Step 7: concatenate heads back into D channels and mix them with the output projection.
        per_head_out = per_head_out.transpose(1, 2)                    # (B, T, H, Dh)
        merged = per_head_out.reshape(B, T, D)                         # (B, T, D)
        return self.o(merged)                                          # (B, T, D)


# =====================================================================================================
# SwiGLU feed-forward
# =====================================================================================================
class SwiGLU(nn.Module):
    """Gated feed-forward block used in Llama / PaLM.

        out = W2( silu(W1 x)  *  W3 x )
                  ^ gate         ^ value

    Input:  x (B, T, D)
    Output: (B, T, D)
    """

    def __init__(self, d, hidden):
        super().__init__()
        self.w1 = nn.Linear(d, hidden, bias=False)    # gate projection
        self.w3 = nn.Linear(d, hidden, bias=False)    # value ("up") projection
        self.w2 = nn.Linear(hidden, d, bias=False)    # down projection back to model width

    def forward(self, x):
        gate = F.silu(self.w1(x))                     # (B, T, hidden)  silu(z) = z * sigmoid(z)
        value = self.w3(x)                            # (B, T, hidden)
        gated = gate * value                          # (B, T, hidden)  elementwise gating
        return self.w2(gated)                         # (B, T, D)


# =====================================================================================================
# Transformer block
# =====================================================================================================
class Block(nn.Module):
    """One pre-norm transformer block:

        x = x + attn(norm1(x))
        x = x + mlp(norm2(x))

    Pre-norm (norm inside the residual branch) keeps the residual stream an identity path, which makes deep
    models train stably without careful warmup tricks.

    Input:  x (B, T, D); cos, sin (T, Dh/2); cache dict or None (passed through to attention)
    Output: (B, T, D)
    """

    def __init__(self, d, h):
        super().__init__()
        self.n1 = RMSNorm(d)
        self.attn = Attention(d, h)
        self.n2 = RMSNorm(d)
        self.mlp = SwiGLU(d, 4 * d)

    def forward(self, x, cos, sin, cache=None):
        # Sub-layer 1: attention (tokens exchange information)
        attn_out = self.attn(self.n1(x), cos, sin, cache)              # (B, T, D)
        x = x + attn_out                                               # (B, T, D)

        # Sub-layer 2: MLP (each token processed independently)
        mlp_out = self.mlp(self.n2(x))                                 # (B, T, D)
        x = x + mlp_out                                                # (B, T, D)
        return x


# =====================================================================================================
# Full GPT model
# =====================================================================================================
class GPT(nn.Module):
    """Decoder-only language model: embedding -> L blocks -> final norm -> lm_head (tied to the embedding).

    Input:  idx (B, T) token ids
            caches: None, or a list with one dict per layer (KV cache)
            pos: absolute position of idx[:, 0] (needed when decoding with a cache so RoPE uses the right angles)
    Output: logits (B, T, vocab)
    """

    def __init__(self, vocab, d=64, h=4, L=2, max_T=256):
        super().__init__()
        self.emb = nn.Embedding(vocab, d)
        self.blocks = nn.ModuleList(Block(d, h) for _ in range(L))
        self.norm = RMSNorm(d)
        self.head = nn.Linear(d, vocab, bias=False)

        # Weight tying: the output projection reuses the embedding matrix (both are (vocab, d)).
        # Saves vocab * d parameters and usually helps small models.
        self.head.weight = self.emb.weight

        # RoPE tables computed once for all positions; buffers move with .to(device) but are not saved in state_dict.
        cos, sin = rope_cache(max_T, d // h)                           # each (max_T, Dh/2)
        self.register_buffer("cos", cos, persistent=False)
        self.register_buffer("sin", sin, persistent=False)

    def forward(self, idx, caches=None, pos=0):
        T = idx.shape[1]

        # Step 1: pick the rotation angles for absolute positions pos .. pos + T - 1
        cos = self.cos[pos:pos + T]                                    # (T, Dh/2)
        sin = self.sin[pos:pos + T]                                    # (T, Dh/2)

        # Step 2: token embeddings (no absolute position embedding -- RoPE handles position inside attention)
        x = self.emb(idx)                                              # (B, T, D)

        # Step 3: run the blocks, giving each layer its own cache dict
        for layer_index, block in enumerate(self.blocks):
            if caches is None:
                layer_cache = None
            else:
                layer_cache = caches[layer_index]
            x = block(x, cos, sin, layer_cache)                        # (B, T, D)

        # Step 4: final norm and projection to vocabulary logits
        x = self.norm(x)                                               # (B, T, D)
        logits = self.head(x)                                          # (B, T, vocab)
        return logits


# =====================================================================================================
# Sampling
# =====================================================================================================
def sample_next(logits, temperature=1.0, top_k=None, top_p=None):
    """Pick the next token from the logits of the last position.

    temperature == 0 -> greedy (argmax). Otherwise:
      1. divide logits by temperature (<1 sharper, >1 flatter)
      2. top-k: keep only the k largest logits
      3. top-p (nucleus): keep the smallest set of most-likely tokens whose total probability >= top_p
      4. sample from the softmax of what is left

    Input:  logits (B, V)
    Output: next token ids (B, 1)
    """
    # Greedy decoding: deterministic, used to compare cached vs non-cached generation.
    if temperature == 0:
        return logits.argmax(dim=-1, keepdim=True)                     # (B, 1)

    # Step 1: temperature scaling
    logits = logits / temperature                                      # (B, V)

    # Step 2: top-k filtering -- everything smaller than the k-th largest logit gets -inf (probability 0)
    if top_k is not None:
        top_values = torch.topk(logits, top_k, dim=-1).values          # (B, k) sorted descending
        kth_largest = top_values[..., -1:]                             # (B, 1)
        logits = logits.masked_fill(logits < kth_largest, float("-inf"))   # (B, V)

    # Step 3: top-p (nucleus) filtering
    if top_p is not None:
        # 3a: sort tokens from most to least likely
        sorted_logits, sorted_idx = logits.sort(dim=-1, descending=True)   # each (B, V)
        sorted_probs = sorted_logits.softmax(dim=-1)                   # (B, V)

        # 3b: probability mass of all tokens strictly BEFORE each token in the sorted list
        cumulative_probs = sorted_probs.cumsum(dim=-1)                 # (B, V) mass including this token
        mass_before = cumulative_probs - sorted_probs                  # (B, V) mass excluding this token

        # 3c: drop a token if the tokens before it already reach top_p. This keeps the smallest prefix whose
        #     mass is >= top_p, and always keeps the single most likely token (its mass_before is 0).
        remove = mass_before > top_p                                   # (B, V) bool
        sorted_logits = sorted_logits.masked_fill(remove, float("-inf"))   # (B, V)

        # 3d: undo the sort -- write each filtered logit back to its original vocabulary index
        unsorted = torch.full_like(logits, float("-inf"))              # (B, V)
        logits = unsorted.scatter(-1, sorted_idx, sorted_logits)       # (B, V)

    # Step 4: sample one token from the remaining distribution
    probs = logits.softmax(dim=-1)                                     # (B, V)
    return torch.multinomial(probs, num_samples=1)                     # (B, 1)


@torch.no_grad()
def generate_naive(model, idx, n, **kw):
    """Generate n tokens WITHOUT a KV cache: every step re-runs the model on the whole sequence so far.

    Simple but O(T^2) work per step. Used as the reference for generate_kv.

    Input:  idx (B, T0) prompt ids; kw are passed to sample_next
    Output: (B, T0 + n)
    """
    for _ in range(n):
        all_logits = model(idx)                                        # (B, T, V)
        last_logits = all_logits[:, -1]                                # (B, V)  only the last position matters
        next_token = sample_next(last_logits, **kw)                    # (B, 1)
        idx = torch.cat([idx, next_token], dim=1)                      # (B, T + 1)
    return idx


@torch.no_grad()
def generate_kv(model, idx, n, **kw):
    """Generate n tokens WITH a KV cache.

    Phase 1 (prefill): run the whole prompt once; every layer stores its k, v.
    Phase 2 (decode):  feed only the newest token; it attends to the cached k, v of all previous tokens.

    Input:  idx (B, T0) prompt ids; kw are passed to sample_next
    Output: (B, T0 + n)  -- identical to generate_naive for greedy decoding
    """
    # One empty cache dict per layer; Attention fills in "k" and "v".
    caches = []
    for _ in model.blocks:
        caches.append({})

    # Phase 1: prefill the prompt (positions 0 .. T0-1)
    prompt_logits = model(idx, caches, pos=0)                          # (B, T0, V)
    logits = prompt_logits[:, -1]                                      # (B, V)

    # Phase 2: decode one token at a time
    out = idx
    for _ in range(n):
        next_token = sample_next(logits, **kw)                         # (B, 1)
        out = torch.cat([out, next_token], dim=1)                      # (B, T + 1)

        # The new token sits at absolute position (current length - 1); RoPE needs that position.
        new_token_pos = out.shape[1] - 1
        step_logits = model(next_token, caches, pos=new_token_pos)     # (B, 1, V)
        logits = step_logits[:, -1]                                    # (B, V)
    return out


# =====================================================================================================
# Optimisation helpers
# =====================================================================================================
def lr_at(step, max_lr, warmup, total, min_lr_ratio=0.1):
    """Learning rate schedule: linear warmup, then cosine decay down to min_lr_ratio * max_lr.

    Input:  step (int), max_lr, warmup steps, total steps
    Output: learning rate (float) for this step
    """
    # Phase 1: linear warmup from max_lr/warmup up to max_lr (avoids huge early updates while Adam stats are noisy)
    if step < warmup:
        return max_lr * (step + 1) / warmup

    # Phase 2: cosine decay. progress goes 0 -> 1 over the remaining steps.
    progress = (step - warmup) / max(1, total - warmup)
    cosine = 0.5 * (1 + math.cos(math.pi * progress))                  # 1 -> 0
    min_lr = max_lr * min_lr_ratio
    return min_lr + (max_lr - min_lr) * cosine


def configure_adamw(model, lr, wd=0.1):
    """AdamW with weight decay applied only to >=2D parameters (matmul weights, embeddings).

    1D parameters (norm gains, biases) are not decayed: shrinking them toward 0 hurts and gives no
    regularisation benefit.

    Output: torch.optim.AdamW with two param groups
    """
    decay_params = []
    no_decay_params = []
    for _name, param in model.named_parameters():
        if param.dim() >= 2:
            decay_params.append(param)
        else:
            no_decay_params.append(param)

    param_groups = [
        {"params": decay_params, "weight_decay": wd},
        {"params": no_decay_params, "weight_decay": 0.0},
    ]
    return torch.optim.AdamW(param_groups, lr=lr, betas=(0.9, 0.95))


# =====================================================================================================
# Tests
# =====================================================================================================
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # ---- data: char-level tokenisation of a tiny repeated text
    text = "the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. " * 50
    chars = sorted(set(text))
    stoi = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(chars)}
    data = torch.tensor([stoi[c] for c in text])

    model = GPT(len(chars))
    opt = configure_adamw(model, 3e-3)

    # ---- training loop
    T = 32          # context length
    B = 16          # micro-batch size
    steps = 300
    accum = 2       # gradient accumulation steps -> effective batch = B * accum
    first = None
    for step in range(steps):
        # set this step's learning rate on every param group
        for group in opt.param_groups:
            group["lr"] = lr_at(step, 3e-3, 30, steps)

        opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        for _ in range(accum):
            # random windows; targets are the inputs shifted left by one
            starts = torch.randint(0, len(data) - T - 1, (B,))
            x = torch.stack([data[i:i + T] for i in starts])             # (B, T)
            y = torch.stack([data[i + 1:i + T + 1] for i in starts])     # (B, T)
            logits = model(x)                                            # (B, T, V)
            # divide by accum so the summed gradients equal the gradient of the mean loss over B * accum
            loss = F.cross_entropy(logits.flatten(0, 1), y.flatten()) / accum
            loss.backward()

        torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(model.parameters(), 1.0)          # clip global grad norm to 1
        opt.step()

        if first is None:
            first = loss.item() * accum
        if step % 100 == 0:
            print(f"step {step} loss {loss.item() * accum:.3f}")
    final = loss.item() * accum
    print(f"loss {first:.3f} -> {final:.3f}")
    assert final < first * 0.3

    # ---- KV-cache generation must equal full-recompute generation (greedy)
    model.eval()
    prompt = torch.tensor([[stoi[c] for c in "the quick "]])
    a = generate_naive(model, prompt, 30, temperature=0)
    b = generate_kv(model, prompt, 30, temperature=0)

    def decode(t):
        return "".join(chars[i] for i in t[0].tolist())

    print("naive:", repr(decode(a)))
    print("kv   :", repr(decode(b)))
    assert torch.equal(a, b)

    torch.manual_seed(1)
    print("top-p:", repr(decode(generate_kv(model, prompt, 30, temperature=0.8, top_k=10, top_p=0.9))))
    print("OK")
05_optimizers_numpy.py — SGD / Adam / AdamW from scratch (numpy, vs torch) download · 
"""SGD+momentum, Adam, AdamW from scratch; checked against torch.optim on the same problem.

Interview checklist:
- Adam: m = b1 m + (1-b1) g ; v = b2 v + (1-b2) g^2 ; bias-correct mhat = m/(1-b1^t), vhat = v/(1-b2^t)
- update: p -= lr * mhat / (sqrt(vhat) + eps)
- L2 in Adam (add wd*p to g) != AdamW (p -= lr*wd*p separately): with Adam the decay gets rescaled by 1/sqrt(v)
- torch SGD momentum: buf = mu*buf + g ; p -= lr*buf (no dampening by default)
"""
import numpy as np


class SGD:
    """SGD with (optional) momentum and L2 weight decay, matching torch.optim.SGD.

    Per parameter p with gradient g:
        g   = g + wd * p          # L2 weight decay folded into the gradient
        buf = mu * buf + g        # momentum buffer (velocity)
        p   = p - lr * buf
    With momentum=0 this is plain gradient descent.
    Parameters are numpy arrays updated IN PLACE (so the caller's arrays change).
    """

    def __init__(self, params, lr, momentum=0.0, weight_decay=0.0):
        """params: list of numpy arrays to optimise."""
        self.p = params
        self.lr = lr
        self.mu = momentum
        self.wd = weight_decay

        # One velocity buffer per parameter, same shape, starts at zero.
        self.buf = []
        for param in params:
            self.buf.append(np.zeros_like(param))

    def step(self, grads):
        """Apply one update. grads: list of arrays, same order/shapes as params."""
        for param, grad, velocity in zip(self.p, grads, self.buf):
            # Step 1: L2 weight decay = adding wd * p to the gradient.
            grad_with_decay = grad + self.wd * param

            # Step 2: momentum, buf = mu * buf + g (in place so self.buf stays updated).
            velocity *= self.mu
            velocity += grad_with_decay

            # Step 3: move the parameter against the velocity (in place).
            param -= self.lr * velocity


class Adam:
    """Adam (and AdamW when decoupled=True), matching torch.optim.Adam / AdamW.

    Per parameter p with gradient g, at step t (starting from 1):
        Adam  (L2):        g = g + wd * p
        AdamW (decoupled): p = p - lr * wd * p        (decay applied directly to the weights)
        m    = b1 * m + (1 - b1) * g                  first moment  (mean of g)
        v    = b2 * v + (1 - b2) * g^2                second moment (mean of g^2)
        mhat = m / (1 - b1^t)                         bias correction (m, v start at 0)
        vhat = v / (1 - b2^t)
        p    = p - lr * mhat / (sqrt(vhat) + eps)
    """

    def __init__(self, params, lr=1e-3, betas=(0.9, 0.999), eps=1e-8, weight_decay=0.0, decoupled=False):
        self.p = params
        self.lr = lr
        self.b1 = betas[0]
        self.b2 = betas[1]
        self.eps = eps
        self.wd = weight_decay
        self.decoupled = decoupled
        self.t = 0                       # number of steps taken so far

        # First and second moment estimates, one per parameter, start at zero.
        self.m = []
        self.v = []
        for param in params:
            self.m.append(np.zeros_like(param))
            self.v.append(np.zeros_like(param))

    def step(self, grads):
        """Apply one Adam/AdamW update to every parameter (in place)."""
        self.t += 1
        bias_correction1 = 1 - self.b1 ** self.t
        bias_correction2 = 1 - self.b2 ** self.t

        for param, grad, m, v in zip(self.p, grads, self.m, self.v):
            # Step 1: weight decay. The two variants differ ONLY here.
            if self.decoupled:
                # AdamW: shrink weights directly; NOT rescaled by 1/sqrt(v).
                param -= self.lr * self.wd * param
            else:
                # Adam + L2: decay goes through the gradient, so it gets rescaled by Adam.
                grad = grad + self.wd * param

            # Step 2: update the running moments (m[:] = ... writes into the stored buffer).
            m[:] = self.b1 * m + (1 - self.b1) * grad
            v[:] = self.b2 * v + (1 - self.b2) * grad * grad

            # Step 3: bias correction. Early on m, v are biased towards 0 because they start at 0.
            m_hat = m / bias_correction1
            v_hat = v / bias_correction2

            # Step 4: per-coordinate adaptive step: big where grads are consistent, small where noisy.
            update = m_hat / (np.sqrt(v_hat) + self.eps)
            param -= self.lr * update


def AdamW(params, lr=1e-3, betas=(0.9, 0.999), eps=1e-8, weight_decay=1e-2):
    """AdamW = Adam with decoupled weight decay (default wd = 0.01, like torch)."""
    return Adam(params, lr, betas, eps, weight_decay, decoupled=True)


def quad_grad(w, A, b):
    """Gradient of the quadratic loss = 0.5 w^T A w - b^T w  (A symmetric).

    grad = A w - b. Input w (n,), A (n, n), b (n,). Output (n,).
    """
    return A @ w - b


if __name__ == "__main__":
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    M = rng.normal(size=(5, 5)); A = M @ M.T + np.eye(5); b = rng.normal(size=5)
    w0 = rng.normal(size=5)

    import torch
    configs = [
        ("SGD+mom", lambda p: SGD(p, 0.01, momentum=0.9, weight_decay=0.01),
         lambda p: torch.optim.SGD(p, 0.01, momentum=0.9, weight_decay=0.01)),
        ("Adam+L2", lambda p: Adam(p, 0.05, weight_decay=0.1),
         lambda p: torch.optim.Adam(p, 0.05, weight_decay=0.1)),
        ("AdamW", lambda p: AdamW(p, 0.05, weight_decay=0.1),
         lambda p: torch.optim.AdamW(p, 0.05, weight_decay=0.1)),
    ]
    for name, mine, ref in configs:
        w = w0.copy(); opt = mine([w])
        wt = torch.tensor(w0, requires_grad=True); topt = ref([wt])
        At, bt = torch.tensor(A), torch.tensor(b)
        for _ in range(200):
            opt.step([quad_grad(w, A, b)])
            topt.zero_grad(); (0.5 * wt @ At @ wt - bt @ wt).backward(); topt.step()
        diff = np.abs(w - wt.detach().numpy()).max()
        print(f"{name:8s} max diff vs torch {diff:.2e}")
        assert diff < 1e-8
    print("OK")
06_debug_training.py — Debug drill: buggy vs fixed training loop (torch) download · 
"""Debug drill: a buggy training loop with 6 planted bugs, followed by the fixed version.

Try first: read `buggy_train` and list every bug before scrolling to `fixed_train`.
Planted bugs:
  1. optimizer.zero_grad() missing  -> gradients accumulate across steps
  2. softmax applied before F.cross_entropy (which already does log_softmax) -> double softmax, weak grads
  3. labels flattened in a different order than logits (yb.t().reshape(-1)) -> silent misalignment
  4. causal mask inverted (masks the past instead of the future) -> model can cheat / or can't learn
  5. model.eval() / torch.no_grad() missing in evaluation (dropout active during eval)
  6. data never shuffled + learning rate 10x too high
Common real-world extras to mention: NaN from log(0), wrong dtype for labels (float vs long),
forgetting .detach() when logging (memory leak), loss averaged twice, train/eval data leakage.
"""
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

torch.manual_seed(0)

VOCAB_SIZE = 10
BATCH_SIZE = 64


def make_data(n=2000, T=8, V=10):
    """Build a toy "predict the previous token" dataset.

    Task: at position t the target is the input token at position t-1
    (position 0 has target 0). Solving it requires attending to the PAST only,
    which is exactly what a correct causal mask allows.

    Args:
        n: number of sequences.
        T: sequence length.
        V: vocabulary size. Input tokens are drawn from 1..V-1 (0 is reserved
           as the "no previous token" label for position 0).

    Returns:
        x: (n, T) long tensor of input tokens.
        y: (n, T) long tensor of targets, y[:, t] = x[:, t-1], y[:, 0] = 0.
    """
    # Step 1: random input tokens in [1, V).
    x = torch.randint(1, V, (n, T))                                  # (n, T)

    # Step 2: target = input shifted right by one position.
    first_column_label = torch.zeros(n, 1, dtype=torch.long)         # (n, 1) label for position 0
    previous_tokens = x[:, :-1]                                      # (n, T-1) tokens 0..T-2
    y = torch.cat([first_column_label, previous_tokens], dim=1)      # (n, T)
    return x, y


class TinyAttnModel(nn.Module):
    """One-layer, single-head self-attention model with a residual connection.

    forward: tokens (B, T) -> logits (B, T, V)

    If `invert_mask=True` the causal mask is flipped (BUG 4): each position can
    only see the FUTURE (strictly later tokens) instead of itself + the past,
    so the "copy previous token" task becomes impossible.
    """

    def __init__(self, V=10, d=32, T=8, invert_mask=False):
        """Create layers.

        Args:
            V: vocab size.  d: model width.  T: max sequence length.
            invert_mask: plant BUG 4 (inverted causal mask) when True.
        """
        super().__init__()
        # NOTE: layers are created in this exact order so the random init
        # (and therefore the printed accuracies) stays reproducible.
        self.emb = nn.Embedding(V, d)            # token embedding
        self.pos = nn.Embedding(T, d)            # learned position embedding
        self.qkv = nn.Linear(d, 3 * d)           # projects h to [q | k | v] in one matmul
        self.out = nn.Linear(d, V)               # final projection to vocab logits
        self.drop = nn.Dropout(0.3)
        self.invert = invert_mask

    def forward(self, x):
        """Run single-head causal self-attention.

        Args:
            x: (B, T) long tensor of token ids.
        Returns:
            logits: (B, T, V) unnormalised scores (NOT softmaxed).

        Formula: att = softmax(mask(Q K^T / sqrt(d))),  out = W_out(dropout(att V) + h)
        """
        B, T = x.shape

        # Step 1: token + position embeddings.
        positions = torch.arange(T)                                  # (T,)
        h = self.emb(x) + self.pos(positions)                        # (B, T, d)

        # Step 2: project to queries, keys, values.
        qkv = self.qkv(h)                                            # (B, T, 3d)
        q, k, v = qkv.chunk(3, dim=-1)                               # each (B, T, d)

        # Step 3: scaled dot-product scores. Scale by sqrt(d) so the softmax
        # does not saturate when d is large.
        d_head = q.shape[-1]
        scores = q @ k.transpose(-1, -2)                             # (B, T, T)
        scores = scores / d_head ** 0.5                              # (B, T, T)

        # Step 4: causal mask. allowed[i, j] = True means "query i may look at key j".
        # Correct version: lower-triangular -> position i sees positions 0..i.
        allowed = torch.ones(T, T, dtype=torch.bool).tril()         # (T, T)
        if self.invert:
            # BUG 4: flipping the mask means position i only sees j > i (the future).
            # For "copy previous token" this hides exactly the token we need.
            allowed = ~allowed

        # Step 5: block disallowed positions with a huge negative number, then softmax.
        scores = scores.masked_fill(~allowed, -1e9)                  # (B, T, T)
        attn_weights = scores.softmax(dim=-1)                        # (B, T, T), rows sum to 1

        # Step 6: weighted sum of values, dropout, residual, project to vocab.
        attended = attn_weights @ v                                  # (B, T, d)
        attended = self.drop(attended)                               # (B, T, d)
        residual = attended + h                                      # (B, T, d)
        logits = self.out(residual)                                  # (B, T, V)
        return logits


def accuracy(model, x, y, fixed):
    """Token-level accuracy of `model` on (x, y).

    Args:
        model: TinyAttnModel.
        x, y: (N, T) inputs and targets.
        fixed: True -> correct evaluation (eval mode + no_grad).
               False -> BUG 5: evaluate in train mode with grads enabled.
    Returns:
        float in [0, 1].
    """
    if fixed:
        # Correct: eval() turns dropout off; no_grad() skips building the
        # autograd graph (saves memory/time). Switch back to train() afterwards.
        model.eval()
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits = model(x)                                        # (N, T, V)
            predictions = logits.argmax(dim=-1)                      # (N, T)
        model.train()
    else:
        # BUG 5: no model.eval() / no torch.no_grad().
        # Dropout is still active -> noisy, pessimistic accuracy,
        # and an autograd graph is built for nothing.
        logits = model(x)                                            # (N, T, V)
        predictions = logits.argmax(dim=-1)                          # (N, T)

    is_correct = (predictions == y).float()                          # (N, T) of 0/1
    return is_correct.mean().item()


def buggy_train(steps=300):
    """Training loop that contains ALL the planted bugs. Returns final accuracy.

    Bugs in here (numbers match the module docstring):
      BUG 1: no optimizer.zero_grad()
      BUG 2: softmax before F.cross_entropy
      BUG 3: labels flattened in transposed order
      BUG 4: inverted causal mask (invert_mask=True)
      BUG 5: evaluation without eval()/no_grad() (fixed=False)
      BUG 6: lr 10x+ too high, and sequential (unshuffled) batches
    """
    x, y = make_data()

    # BUG 4: model built with the inverted causal mask.
    model = TinyAttnModel(invert_mask=True)

    # BUG 6a: lr = 0.1 is far too high for Adam on this model (typical: 1e-3..3e-3).
    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=1e-1)

    for step in range(steps):
        # BUG 6b: batches are taken in a fixed sequential order, never shuffled.
