        # (Harmless on this i.i.d. toy data, but in general it biases the gradients
        # if the dataset is sorted, e.g. by length or by label.)
        start = (step * BATCH_SIZE) % 1900
        end = start + BATCH_SIZE
        xb = x[start:end]                                            # (B, T)
        yb = y[start:end]                                            # (B, T)

        # BUG 2: softmax here, and F.cross_entropy applies log_softmax AGAIN.
        # The "logits" become probabilities in [0, 1], so the second softmax is
        # nearly uniform and the gradients are tiny.
        probs = model(xb).softmax(dim=-1)                            # (B, T, V)

        # BUG 3: logits are flattened in (B, T) order, but yb.t() is (T, B),
        # so labels are flattened in (T, B) order -> each prediction is
        # compared against the label of a DIFFERENT token. No error is raised
        # because the total element count still matches.
        flat_logits = probs.view(-1, VOCAB_SIZE)                     # (B*T, V) in (b, t) order
        flat_labels = yb.t().reshape(-1)                             # (B*T,)   in (t, b) order  <-- wrong
        loss = F.cross_entropy(flat_logits, flat_labels)

        # BUG 1: no optimizer.zero_grad() -> .grad keeps summing over all
        # previous steps, so the effective update grows and points in a stale direction.
        loss.backward()
        optimizer.step()

    # BUG 5: evaluate without eval()/no_grad().
    return accuracy(model, x, y, fixed=False)


def fixed_train(steps=300):
    """Correct training loop. Returns final accuracy (should be ~1.0).

    Fixes vs buggy_train:
      FIX 1: optimizer.zero_grad(set_to_none=True) every step
      FIX 2: pass raw logits to F.cross_entropy (it does log_softmax internally)
      FIX 3: flatten logits and labels in the same (B, T) order
      FIX 4: correct lower-triangular causal mask
      FIX 5: evaluate with model.eval() + torch.no_grad()
      FIX 6: lr = 3e-3, random mini-batches; plus gradient clipping for safety
    """
    x, y = make_data()

    # FIX 4: normal causal mask.
    model = TinyAttnModel(invert_mask=False)

    # FIX 6a: sensible Adam learning rate.
    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=3e-3)

    for step in range(steps):
        # FIX 6b: sample a random mini-batch each step (shuffling).
        batch_indices = torch.randint(0, len(x), (BATCH_SIZE,))      # (B,)
        xb = x[batch_indices]                                        # (B, T)
        yb = y[batch_indices]                                        # (B, T)

        # FIX 2: keep the raw logits; cross_entropy does log_softmax itself.
        logits = model(xb)                                           # (B, T, V)

        # FIX 3: flatten both tensors the same way, so row i of logits
        # lines up with element i of labels.
        flat_logits = logits.reshape(-1, VOCAB_SIZE)                 # (B*T, V)
        flat_labels = yb.reshape(-1)                                 # (B*T,)
        loss = F.cross_entropy(flat_logits, flat_labels)

        # FIX 1: clear old gradients BEFORE backward.
        optimizer.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        loss.backward()

        # Extra safety: clip the global gradient norm to avoid rare spikes.
        torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(model.parameters(), max_norm=1.0)
        optimizer.step()

    # FIX 5: proper evaluation (dropout off, no autograd graph).
    return accuracy(model, x, y, fixed=True)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    buggy_acc = buggy_train()
    fixed_acc = fixed_train()
    print(f"buggy acc {buggy_acc:.3f}   fixed acc {fixed_acc:.3f}")
    assert fixed_acc > 0.95 and fixed_acc > buggy_acc
    print("OK")
07_classic_ml_numpy.py — Classic ML: logreg, k-means, PCA, kNN, conv2d im2col (numpy) download · 
"""Classic ML from scratch: logistic regression (GD), k-means (k-means++ init), PCA via SVD, kNN, conv2d (im2col).

Interview checklist:
- logistic regression grad: X^T (sigmoid(Xw) - y) / N ; use a stable sigmoid / logaddexp for the loss
- k-means: alternate assign / update; handle empty clusters; k-means++ init; objective is non-increasing
- PCA: center X, SVD X = U S V^T, components = V[:k], explained variance = S^2 / (N-1)
- conv2d via im2col: (N,C,H,W) -> patches (N*Ho*Wo, C*kh*kw) @ W.reshape(F, -1).T
"""
import numpy as np


def sigmoid(z):
    """Numerically stable sigmoid, elementwise. Same shape in and out.

    sigmoid(z) = 1 / (1 + exp(-z))
    For z >= 0 use 1 / (1 + exp(-|z|)); for z < 0 use the equivalent exp(z) / (1 + exp(z)).
    Both forms only ever compute exp(-|z|) <= 1, so nothing overflows.
    """
    exp_neg_abs = np.exp(-np.abs(z))                    # in (0, 1], never overflows
    positive_branch = 1 / (1 + exp_neg_abs)             # correct for z >= 0
    negative_branch = exp_neg_abs / (1 + exp_neg_abs)   # correct for z < 0
    return np.where(z >= 0, positive_branch, negative_branch)


def logreg_fit(X, y, lr=0.5, steps=1000, l2=0.0):
    """Binary logistic regression trained with full-batch gradient descent.

    Input:  X (N, D), y (N,) in {0, 1}
    Output: w (D+1,) weights with the bias as the LAST entry, final loss (scalar)

    Model:  p = sigmoid(Xb @ w), Xb = [X, 1]
    Loss:   BCE = mean(log(1 + exp(z)) - y * z),  z = Xb @ w
    Grad:   Xb^T (p - y) / N  +  l2 * w   (bias is not regularised)
    """
    N = len(X)

    # Step 1: append a column of ones so the bias is just another weight.
    ones_column = np.ones(N)                            # (N,)
    Xb = np.c_[X, ones_column]                          # (N, D+1)
    w = np.zeros(Xb.shape[1])                           # (D+1,)

    for _ in range(steps):
        # Step 2: forward -> logits and probabilities.
        z = Xb @ w                                      # (N,)
        probs = sigmoid(z)                              # (N,)

        # Step 3: gradient of mean BCE w.r.t. w.
        error = probs - y                               # (N,)
        data_grad = Xb.T @ error / N                    # (D+1,)

        # Step 4: L2 penalty on the weights but NOT on the bias (last entry -> 0).
        w_without_bias = np.r_[w[:-1], 0]               # (D+1,)
        reg_grad = l2 * w_without_bias                  # (D+1,)

        # Step 5: gradient descent step.
        grad = data_grad + reg_grad                     # (D+1,)
        w -= lr * grad

    # Stable BCE: log(1 + e^z) = logaddexp(0, z). Uses z from the last iteration.
    loss = np.mean(np.logaddexp(0, z) - y * z)
    return w, loss


def kmeans_single(X, k, iters=100, seed=0):
    """One run of k-means (Lloyd's algorithm) with k-means++ initialisation.

    Input:  X (N, D), k clusters
    Output: centers (k, D), assignments (N,), objective = sum of squared distances to assigned center
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    N = len(X)

    # ---- Step 1: k-means++ initialisation ----
    # First center: a uniformly random point.
    first_index = rng.integers(N)
    centers_list = [X[first_index]]
    # Every next center: sample a point with probability proportional to its squared
    # distance to the nearest center chosen so far -> centers end up spread out.
    for _ in range(1, k):
        current_centers = np.array(centers_list)                        # (j, D)
        diff = X[:, None, :] - current_centers[None, :, :]              # (N, j, D)
        dist_sq_to_each = (diff ** 2).sum(axis=-1)                      # (N, j)
        dist_sq_to_nearest = dist_sq_to_each.min(axis=1)                # (N,)
        sample_probs = dist_sq_to_nearest / dist_sq_to_nearest.sum()    # (N,)
        next_index = rng.choice(N, p=sample_probs)
        centers_list.append(X[next_index])
    centers = np.array(centers_list)                                    # (k, D)

    # ---- Step 2: Lloyd iterations: assign, then update ----
    prev_objective = np.inf
    for _ in range(iters):
        # 2a. Assignment: each point goes to its nearest center.
        diff = X[:, None, :] - centers[None, :, :]                      # (N, k, D)
        dist_sq = (diff ** 2).sum(axis=-1)                              # (N, k)
        assignments = dist_sq.argmin(axis=1)                            # (N,)

        # 2b. Objective (inertia). Must never increase from one iteration to the next.
        objective = dist_sq[np.arange(N), assignments].sum()
        assert objective <= prev_objective + 1e-9

        # 2c. Update: each center moves to the mean of its points.
        for j in range(k):
            members = X[assignments == j]                               # (n_j, D)
            if len(members) > 0:
                centers[j] = members.mean(axis=0)
            else:
                # Empty cluster: re-seed it at a random data point instead of producing NaN.
                centers[j] = X[rng.integers(N)]

        # 2d. Stop when the objective no longer improves.
        if prev_objective - objective < 1e-10:
            break
        prev_objective = objective

    return centers, assignments, objective


def kmeans(X, k, n_init=5, seed=0):
    """k-means only finds a local optimum -> run several inits and keep the lowest objective.

    Output: (centers (k, D), assignments (N,), objective) of the best run.
    """
    best_result = None
    for i in range(n_init):
        result = kmeans_single(X, k, seed=seed + i)
        objective = result[2]
        if best_result is None or objective < best_result[2]:
            best_result = result
    return best_result


def pca(X, k):
    """Principal Component Analysis via SVD.

    Input:  X (N, D), k components to keep
    Output: components (k, D)       top-k directions (rows, unit length)
            projected  (N, k)       data expressed in those directions
            ratio      (k,)         fraction of total variance explained by each component
    """
    # Step 1: center the data (PCA is about variance around the mean).
    mean = X.mean(axis=0)                               # (D,)
    X_centered = X - mean                               # (N, D)

    # Step 2: SVD, X_centered = U S Vt. Rows of Vt are the principal directions,
    # sorted by singular value (largest first).
    U, S, Vt = np.linalg.svd(X_centered, full_matrices=False)   # U (N, r), S (r,), Vt (r, D)

    # Step 3: keep the top-k directions.
    components = Vt[:k]                                 # (k, D)

    # Step 4: variance along each direction = S^2 / (N - 1).
    variance = S ** 2 / (len(X) - 1)                    # (r,)
    explained_ratio = variance[:k] / variance.sum()     # (k,)

    # Step 5: project the centered data onto the components.
    projected = X_centered @ components.T               # (N, k)
    return components, projected, explained_ratio


def knn_predict(Xtr, ytr, Xte, k=5):
    """k-nearest-neighbour classification by majority vote.

    Input:  Xtr (Ntr, D), ytr (Ntr,) non-negative int labels, Xte (Nte, D)
    Output: predictions (Nte,)
    """
    # Step 1: all pairwise squared distances without a Python loop, using
    # ||a - b||^2 = ||a||^2 + ||b||^2 - 2 a.b
    test_sq_norms = (Xte ** 2).sum(axis=1)              # (Nte,)
    train_sq_norms = (Xtr ** 2).sum(axis=1)             # (Ntr,)
    cross_term = Xte @ Xtr.T                            # (Nte, Ntr)
    dist_sq = test_sq_norms[:, None] + train_sq_norms[None, :] - 2 * cross_term   # (Nte, Ntr)

    # Step 2: indices of the k closest training points. argpartition is O(N),
    # cheaper than a full sort; the k neighbours are unordered but that's fine for voting.
    neighbour_idx = np.argpartition(dist_sq, k, axis=1)[:, :k]  # (Nte, k)
    neighbour_labels = ytr[neighbour_idx]                       # (Nte, k)

    # Step 3: majority vote per test point.
    predictions = []
    for labels_of_one_point in neighbour_labels:
        vote_counts = np.bincount(labels_of_one_point)
        predictions.append(vote_counts.argmax())
    return np.array(predictions)


def conv2d_im2col(x, W, b, stride=1, pad=0):
    """2D convolution (cross-correlation, like torch) via im2col + one matrix multiply.

    Input:  x (N, C, H, W_in), W (F, C, kh, kw) filters, b (F,)
    Output: (N, F, Ho, Wo) with Ho = (H + 2*pad - kh) // stride + 1 (same for Wo)

    Idea: copy every receptive-field patch into a row of a big matrix
    (N*Ho*Wo, C*kh*kw), then the convolution is just patches @ filters^T.
    """
    N, C, H, W_in = x.shape
    F, _, kh, kw = W.shape

    # Step 1: zero-pad height and width only.
    x_padded = np.pad(x, ((0, 0), (0, 0), (pad, pad), (pad, pad)))     # (N, C, H+2p, W+2p)

    # Step 2: output spatial size.
    Ho = (H + 2 * pad - kh) // stride + 1
    Wo = (W_in + 2 * pad - kw) // stride + 1

    # Step 3: build the patch tensor. Loop over the kh*kw kernel offsets (small),
    # not over output pixels (large). For offset (i, j), the strided slice picks the
    # input pixel that sits at that offset inside every output position's window.
    cols = np.empty((N, Ho, Wo, C, kh, kw))
    for i in range(kh):
        for j in range(kw):
            row_end = i + stride * Ho
            col_end = j + stride * Wo
            window_pixels = x_padded[:, :, i:row_end:stride, j:col_end:stride]   # (N, C, Ho, Wo)
            cols[:, :, :, :, i, j] = window_pixels.transpose(0, 2, 3, 1)        # (N, Ho, Wo, C)

    # Step 4: flatten patches and filters so the conv becomes one matmul.
    patches = cols.reshape(N * Ho * Wo, C * kh * kw)    # (N*Ho*Wo, C*kh*kw)
    filters = W.reshape(F, C * kh * kw)                 # (F, C*kh*kw)
    out = patches @ filters.T + b                       # (N*Ho*Wo, F)

    # Step 5: reshape back to image layout (N, F, Ho, Wo).
    out = out.reshape(N, Ho, Wo, F)                     # (N, Ho, Wo, F)
    out = out.transpose(0, 3, 1, 2)                     # (N, F, Ho, Wo)
    return out


if __name__ == "__main__":
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    X = np.r_[rng.normal(-2, 1, (200, 2)), rng.normal(2, 1, (200, 2))]
    y = np.r_[np.zeros(200), np.ones(200)]
    w, loss = logreg_fit(X, y)
    acc = ((np.c_[X, np.ones(400)] @ w > 0) == y).mean()
    print(f"logreg loss {loss:.3f} acc {acc:.3f}"); assert acc > 0.95

    blobs = np.r_[rng.normal(0, .3, (100, 2)), rng.normal(3, .3, (100, 2)), rng.normal([0, 3], .3, (100, 2))]
    C, a, obj = kmeans(blobs, 3)
    C = C[np.argsort(C[:, 0] + 10 * C[:, 1])]
    print("kmeans centers\n", np.round(C, 2)); assert np.allclose(C, [[0, 0], [0, 3], [3, 3]], atol=0.15)

    Z = rng.normal(size=(500, 2)) @ np.array([[3, 0, 0], [0, 1, 0]]) + 0.01 * rng.normal(size=(500, 3))
    comps, proj, ratio = pca(Z, 2)
    print("pca explained ratio", np.round(ratio, 3)); assert ratio.sum() > 0.99

    pred = knn_predict(X, y.astype(int), X[::10])
    print("knn acc", (pred == y[::10]).mean())

    import torch
    x = rng.normal(size=(2, 3, 7, 7)); Wc = rng.normal(size=(4, 3, 3, 3)); bc = rng.normal(size=4)
    ref = torch.nn.functional.conv2d(torch.tensor(x), torch.tensor(Wc), torch.tensor(bc), stride=2, padding=1).numpy()
    out = conv2d_im2col(x, Wc, bc, stride=2, pad=1)
    print("conv2d max diff vs torch", np.abs(out - ref).max()); assert np.allclose(out, ref)
    print("OK")
08_rl_losses_torch.py — RL losses: REINFORCE, GAE, PPO, GRPO, k3 KL (torch) download · 
"""RL for LLM agents: REINFORCE with baseline, GAE, PPO clipped loss, GRPO group advantage, k3 KL estimator.
Plus a runnable sanity check: REINFORCE learns a 5-armed bandit.

Interview checklist:
- policy gradient: grad E[R] = E[grad log pi(a|s) * A]; baseline reduces variance, keeps it unbiased
- GAE: delta_t = r_t + gamma V(s_{t+1}) (1-done) - V(s_t); A_t = delta_t + gamma*lam*(1-done) A_{t+1}
- PPO: ratio = exp(logp_new - logp_old); L = -min(ratio*A, clip(ratio, 1-eps, 1+eps)*A)
- GRPO: sample G responses per prompt; A_i = (r_i - mean(r)) / (std(r) + eps); no value net
- token-level masking: only response tokens count; average over valid tokens (be explicit about seq vs token mean)
- KL to reference: k3 = exp(ref - logp) - (ref - logp) - 1  (unbiased, always >= 0)
"""
import torch


def reinforce_loss(logp, returns, baseline=None):
    """REINFORCE (vanilla policy gradient) loss with a baseline.

    Formula:
        A_i  = R_i - b
        loss = -mean_i( log pi(a_i) * A_i )
    Minimising this loss = gradient ascent on expected return, because
    grad E[R] = E[ grad log pi(a) * (R - b) ].

    Args:
        logp:     (N,) log pi(a_i) of the sampled actions (requires grad).
        returns:  (N,) return R_i observed for each sample.
        baseline: optional scalar / (N,) tensor b. Defaults to mean(returns).
                  Any baseline that does not depend on a_i keeps the gradient
                  unbiased but reduces its variance.
    Returns:
        scalar loss tensor.
    """
    # Step 1: pick the baseline (batch mean if none given).
    if baseline is None:
        baseline_value = returns.mean()
    else:
        baseline_value = baseline

    # Step 2: advantage = how much better than "average" this sample was.
    advantage = returns - baseline_value                              # (N,)

    # Step 3: detach the advantage: it is a fixed weight, gradients must only
    # flow through log pi, never through the reward/baseline.
    advantage = advantage.detach()                                    # (N,)

    # Step 4: negative because optimisers minimise.
    weighted_logp = logp * advantage                                  # (N,)
    loss = -weighted_logp.mean()
    return loss


def gae(rewards, values, dones, gamma=0.99, lam=0.95):
    """Generalised Advantage Estimation, computed backwards in time.

    Formulas (nonterm_t = 1 - done_t):
        delta_t = r_t + gamma * V(s_{t+1}) * nonterm_t - V(s_t)        (TD error)
        A_t     = delta_t + gamma * lam * nonterm_t * A_{t+1}
        R_t     = A_t + V(s_t)                                         (value target)
    lam=0 -> one-step TD (low variance, high bias); lam=1 -> Monte Carlo (opposite).

    Args:
        rewards: (T,) reward at each step.
        values:  (T+1,) value estimates V(s_0..s_T); the last entry is the
                 bootstrap value of the state after the final step.
        dones:   (T,) 1.0 if the episode ended at step t, else 0.0.
    Returns:
        advantages: (T,)
        returns:    (T,) = advantages + values[:-1]
    """
    num_steps = rewards.shape[0]
    advantages = torch.zeros(num_steps)                               # (T,)

    # A_{T} = 0 beyond the end of the rollout.
    next_advantage = 0.0

    # Walk backwards so A_{t+1} is already known when computing A_t.
    for t in reversed(range(num_steps)):
        # Step 1: if the episode ended at t, do not bootstrap from s_{t+1}
        # and do not carry advantage across the episode boundary.
        not_terminal = 1.0 - dones[t]

        # Step 2: one-step TD error.
        bootstrapped_next_value = gamma * values[t + 1] * not_terminal
        td_error = rewards[t] + bootstrapped_next_value - values[t]

        # Step 3: exponentially-weighted sum of future TD errors.
        advantage_t = td_error + gamma * lam * not_terminal * next_advantage
        advantages[t] = advantage_t
        next_advantage = advantage_t

    # Value-function regression target.
    returns = advantages + values[:-1]                                # (T,)
    return advantages, returns


def ppo_loss(logp_new, logp_old, adv, mask, clip_eps=0.2):
    """PPO clipped surrogate loss, averaged over valid (response) tokens.

    Formula:
        ratio = exp(logp_new - logp_old)       = pi_new / pi_old
        L_tok = -min( ratio * A, clip(ratio, 1-eps, 1+eps) * A )
        loss  = sum(L_tok * mask) / sum(mask)  (token-level mean)
    Taking the min makes the objective pessimistic: once the ratio moves
    outside [1-eps, 1+eps] in the direction that helps, the gradient is zero,
    so the new policy cannot drift too far from the old one in one update.

    Args:
        logp_new: (B, T) log-probs under the current policy (requires grad).
        logp_old: (B, T) log-probs under the policy that generated the data.
        adv:      (B, T) advantages (per token, or a sequence value broadcast).
        mask:     (B, T) 1 for response tokens, 0 for prompt / padding.
        clip_eps: clip range epsilon.
    Returns:
        scalar loss.
    """
    # Step 1: probability ratio pi_new / pi_old (computed in log space for stability).
    log_ratio = logp_new - logp_old                                   # (B, T)
    ratio = torch.exp(log_ratio)                                      # (B, T)

    # Step 2: the two surrogate objectives.
    unclipped_objective = ratio * adv                                 # (B, T)
    clipped_ratio = ratio.clamp(1 - clip_eps, 1 + clip_eps)           # (B, T)
    clipped_objective = clipped_ratio * adv                           # (B, T)

    # Step 3: pessimistic (minimum) objective, negated to get a loss.
    per_token_loss = -torch.min(unclipped_objective, clipped_objective)  # (B, T)

    # Step 4: average over valid tokens only.
    masked_loss_sum = (per_token_loss * mask).sum()
    num_valid_tokens = mask.sum()
    return masked_loss_sum / num_valid_tokens


def grpo_advantages(rewards, group_size, eps=1e-6):
    """GRPO group-relative advantages (no value network needed).

    For each prompt we sampled G = group_size responses. Each response's
    advantage is its reward standardised within its own group:
        A_i = (r_i - mean(r_group)) / (std(r_group) + eps)

    Args:
        rewards:    (B*G,) rewards, ordered so the G responses of the same
                    prompt are contiguous: [p0_r0..p0_r{G-1}, p1_r0, ...].
        group_size: G.
        eps:        avoids division by zero when every response in a group
                    got the same reward (then all advantages are 0).
    Returns:
        (B*G,) advantages in the same order.
    """
    # Step 1: one row per prompt.
    rewards_per_group = rewards.view(-1, group_size)                  # (B, G)

    # Step 2: per-group statistics.
    group_mean = rewards_per_group.mean(dim=1, keepdim=True)          # (B, 1)
    group_std = rewards_per_group.std(dim=1, keepdim=True)            # (B, 1)  (unbiased std)

    # Step 3: standardise within each group.
    centered = rewards_per_group - group_mean                         # (B, G)
    advantages = centered / (group_std + eps)                         # (B, G)

    # Step 4: back to the original flat layout.
    return advantages.view(-1)                                        # (B*G,)


def k3_kl(logp, logp_ref):
    """Per-sample "k3" estimator of KL(pi || pi_ref), using samples from pi.

    Formula, with d = log pi_ref(x) - log pi(x) = log(ref/pi):
        k3 = exp(d) - d - 1
    Properties:
        - unbiased: E_pi[exp(d)] = 1, so E[k3] = E[-d] = KL(pi || pi_ref)
        - always >= 0 (since e^d >= 1 + d), unlike the naive estimator -d
        - lower variance than -d
    Used in GRPO as the per-token KL penalty to the reference model.

    Args:
        logp:     (...) log pi(x) for samples x ~ pi.
        logp_ref: (...) log pi_ref(x) for the same samples.
    Returns:
        (...) per-sample KL estimates (take .mean() for the KL estimate).
    """
    log_ratio_ref_over_policy = logp_ref - logp                       # (...)
    k3 = torch.exp(log_ratio_ref_over_policy) - log_ratio_ref_over_policy - 1
    return k3


def token_logprobs(logits, tokens):
    """Log-probability of each chosen token.

    Args:
        logits: (B, T, V) model outputs.
        tokens: (B, T) token ids actually generated / observed.
    Returns:
        (B, T) log pi(tokens[b, t]).
    """
    # Step 1: normalise over the vocabulary.
    log_probs_all_vocab = torch.log_softmax(logits, dim=-1)           # (B, T, V)

    # Step 2: pick out the log-prob of the token that was actually used.
    # gather needs an index with the same number of dims -> add a trailing dim.
    token_index = tokens.unsqueeze(-1)                                # (B, T, 1)
    chosen_log_probs = log_probs_all_vocab.gather(-1, token_index)    # (B, T, 1)
    return chosen_log_probs.squeeze(-1)                               # (B, T)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    torch.manual_seed(0)

    # --- 1. REINFORCE on a 5-armed bandit: arm 2 has the highest mean reward ---
    true_means = torch.tensor([0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.3, 0.0])
    policy_logits = torch.zeros(5, requires_grad=True)
    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam([policy_logits], lr=0.05)
    for _ in range(500):
        policy = torch.distributions.Categorical(logits=policy_logits)
        actions = policy.sample((64,))                                # (64,)
        noise = 0.1 * torch.randn(64)
        rewards = true_means[actions] + noise                         # (64,)
        loss = reinforce_loss(policy.log_prob(actions), rewards)
        optimizer.zero_grad()
        loss.backward()
        optimizer.step()
    action_probs = policy_logits.softmax(0)
    print("bandit policy", action_probs.detach().numpy().round(3))
    assert action_probs.argmax() == 2 and action_probs[2] > 0.9

    # --- 2. GAE sanity: lam=1, gamma=1, V=0 -> advantage = reward-to-go ---
    rewards = torch.tensor([1.0, 0.0, 2.0])
    values = torch.zeros(4)
    dones = torch.tensor([0.0, 0.0, 1.0])
    advantages, returns = gae(rewards, values, dones, gamma=1.0, lam=1.0)
    print("GAE", advantages.tolist())
    assert torch.allclose(advantages, torch.tensor([3.0, 2.0, 2.0]))

    # --- 3. PPO: ratio = 1 -> loss = -mean(adv); clipping kicks in for large ratio ---
    logp_same = torch.zeros(2, 3)
    adv = torch.tensor([[1.0, -1.0, 2.0], [0.5, 0.5, 0.0]])
    mask = torch.ones(2, 3)
    assert torch.isclose(ppo_loss(logp_same, logp_same, adv, mask), -(adv.mean()))
    logp_shifted = torch.full((2, 3), 1.0)                            # ratio = e ~ 2.72 > 1.2
    print("PPO clipped loss", ppo_loss(logp_shifted, logp_same, adv, mask).item())

    # --- 4. GRPO: advantages are zero-mean within each group ---
    group_rewards = torch.tensor([1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 0.0])
    grpo_adv = grpo_advantages(group_rewards, 4)
    print("GRPO adv", grpo_adv.numpy().round(3))
    assert torch.allclose(grpo_adv.view(2, 4).mean(1), torch.zeros(2), atol=1e-6)

    # --- 5. k3 KL is >= 0 and ~ true KL on average ---
    policy_dist = torch.distributions.Categorical(logits=torch.randn(10))
    reference_dist = torch.distributions.Categorical(logits=torch.randn(10))
    samples = policy_dist.sample((200000,))
    k3_estimate = k3_kl(policy_dist.log_prob(samples), reference_dist.log_prob(samples)).mean()
    true_kl = torch.distributions.kl_divergence(policy_dist, reference_dist)
    print(f"k3 KL est {k3_estimate:.4f} true {true_kl:.4f}")
    assert abs(k3_estimate - true_kl) < 0.02
    print("OK")
09_sysdesign_snippets.py — System-design snippets: worker pool, UCB, bootstrap, cache, halving download · 
"""Small coding pieces that tend to show up inside a system design round.

1. Async worker pool with timeout + retry + exponential backoff (rollout / eval runner)
2. UCB1 bandit for allocating budget across candidate ideas
3. Paired bootstrap / significance test: is candidate B really better than A?
4. Content-hash cache + dedup for experiment results
5. Successive halving (budgeted search over candidates)
"""
import asyncio
import hashlib
import json
import math
import random


# ---------- 1. worker pool ----------
async def run_with_retry(fn, item, timeout, retries=3, base_delay=0.05):
    """Call `await fn(item)` with a per-attempt timeout, retrying on any failure.

    Retry schedule (exponential backoff with jitter):
        sleep_k = base_delay * 2**k * (1 + U[0,1))      for attempt k = 0, 1, ...
    Jitter spreads retries out so many failing workers don't all hit the
    backend again at the same moment ("thundering herd").

    Args:
        fn:         async function taking one item.
        item:       the work item.
        timeout:    seconds allowed per attempt (asyncio.TimeoutError on expiry).
        retries:    number of extra attempts after the first (total = retries + 1).
        base_delay: first backoff delay in seconds.
    Returns:
        {"item", "ok": True, "result"} on success, or
        {"item", "ok": False, "error": <exception class name>} after the last failure.
        Never raises: one bad item must not crash the whole pool.
    """
    total_attempts = retries + 1
    for attempt in range(total_attempts):
        try:
            # Step 1: run one attempt, cancelled if it exceeds `timeout`.
            result = await asyncio.wait_for(fn(item), timeout)
            return {"item": item, "ok": True, "result": result}
        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001  (TimeoutError included)
            # Step 2: out of attempts -> report the failure instead of raising.
            is_last_attempt = attempt == retries
            if is_last_attempt:
                return {"item": item, "ok": False, "error": type(error).__name__}

            # Step 3: back off before retrying: doubles each attempt, plus jitter.
            exponential_delay = base_delay * 2 ** attempt
            jitter_factor = 1 + random.random()
            await asyncio.sleep(exponential_delay * jitter_factor)


async def worker_pool(fn, items, concurrency=8, timeout=1.0, retries=3):
    """Process all `items` with at most `concurrency` calls in flight at once.

    Args:
        fn:          async function applied to each item.
        items:       iterable of work items.
        concurrency: max simultaneous calls (protects rate limits / GPUs).
        timeout, retries: forwarded to run_with_retry.
    Returns:
        list of result dicts (see run_with_retry), in the SAME order as items.
    """
    # A semaphore with N permits: each task must hold one while running.
    semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(concurrency)

    async def run_one_with_limit(item):
        """Wait for a free slot, then run the item with retries."""
        async with semaphore:
            return await run_with_retry(fn, item, timeout, retries)

    # Create all tasks up front; the semaphore throttles them.
    # gather() preserves the input order in its output.
    tasks = [run_one_with_limit(item) for item in items]
    return await asyncio.gather(*tasks)


# ---------- 2. UCB1 ----------
class UCB1:
    """UCB1 multi-armed bandit.

    Score of arm i at time t:
        UCB_i = mean_reward_i + sqrt( c * ln(t) / n_i )
    The first term exploits arms that look good; the second term (the
    "uncertainty bonus") is big for rarely-pulled arms, forcing exploration.
    Every arm is pulled once before the formula is used (n_i = 0 would divide by zero).

    Attributes:
        n: list[int]   number of pulls per arm.
        s: list[float] sum of rewards per arm.
        c: exploration constant (2.0 = classic UCB1).
        t: total number of select() calls so far.
    """

    def __init__(self, n_arms, c=2.0):
        """Start with zero pulls and zero reward on every arm."""
        self.n = [0] * n_arms
        self.s = [0.0] * n_arms
        self.c = c
        self.t = 0

    def _ucb_score(self, arm):
        """Mean reward of `arm` plus its exploration bonus (requires n[arm] > 0)."""
        mean_reward = self.s[arm] / self.n[arm]
        exploration_bonus = math.sqrt(self.c * math.log(self.t) / self.n[arm])
        return mean_reward + exploration_bonus

    def select(self):
        """Return the index of the arm to pull next."""
        self.t += 1

        # Step 1: make sure every arm has been tried at least once.
        for arm, pull_count in enumerate(self.n):
            if pull_count == 0:
                return arm

        # Step 2: otherwise pick the arm with the highest UCB score.
        best_arm = 0
        best_score = -math.inf
        for arm in range(len(self.n)):
            score = self._ucb_score(arm)
            if score > best_score:
                best_arm = arm
                best_score = score
        return best_arm

    def update(self, i, reward):
        """Record that arm `i` was pulled and returned `reward`."""
        self.n[i] += 1
        self.s[i] += reward


# ---------- 3. paired bootstrap ----------
def paired_bootstrap(scores_a, scores_b, n_boot=5000, seed=0):
    """Paired bootstrap test: is system B really better than A on the same tasks?

    Because both systems ran on the SAME tasks, we work with per-task
    differences d_j = b_j - a_j (pairing removes task-difficulty noise).

    Procedure:
        1. d = [b_j - a_j]
        2. repeat n_boot times: resample n tasks WITH replacement, record mean(d*)
        3. 95% CI = 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles of those bootstrap means
        4. p-value (one-sided) ~ fraction of bootstrap means <= 0

    Args:
        scores_a, scores_b: per-task scores, same length and task order.
        n_boot: number of bootstrap resamples.
        seed:   RNG seed for reproducibility.
    Returns:
        (mean_diff, (ci_low, ci_high), p_value_B_not_better)
    """
    rng = random.Random(seed)

    # Step 1: per-task differences.
    differences = []
    for score_a, score_b in zip(scores_a, scores_b):
        differences.append(score_b - score_a)
    num_tasks = len(differences)

    # Step 2: bootstrap distribution of the mean difference.
    bootstrap_means = []
    for _ in range(n_boot):
        resample_total = 0.0
        for _ in range(num_tasks):
            random_task = rng.randrange(num_tasks)
            resample_total += differences[random_task]
        bootstrap_means.append(resample_total / num_tasks)
    bootstrap_means.sort()

    # Step 3: how often does the resampled mean say "B is not better"?
    num_not_better = 0
    for mean_value in bootstrap_means:
        if mean_value <= 0:
            num_not_better += 1
    p_value = num_not_better / n_boot

    # Step 4: percentile confidence interval.
    ci_low = bootstrap_means[int(0.025 * n_boot)]
    ci_high = bootstrap_means[int(0.975 * n_boot)]

    observed_mean_diff = sum(differences) / num_tasks
    return observed_mean_diff, (ci_low, ci_high), p_value


# ---------- 4. cache ----------
class ResultCache:
    """In-memory cache of experiment results keyed by a content hash.

    The key is sha256 over (code, config, seed), so identical experiments are
    deduplicated and run only once, regardless of who submits them.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        """Create an empty key -> result store."""
        self.store = {}

    @staticmethod
    def key(code: str, config: dict, seed: int) -> str:
        """Deterministic hex hash of everything that determines the result.

        sort_keys=True makes {"a":1,"b":2} and {"b":2,"a":1} hash identically.
        """
        payload = {"code": code, "config": config, "seed": seed}
        canonical_json = json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True)
        return hashlib.sha256(canonical_json.encode()).hexdigest()

    def get_or_run(self, code, config, seed, run):
        """Return the cached result, or call `run()` once and cache what it returns."""
        cache_key = self.key(code, config, seed)
        if cache_key not in self.store:
            # Cache miss: run the experiment (expensive) exactly once.
            self.store[cache_key] = run()
        return self.store[cache_key]


# ---------- 5. successive halving ----------
def successive_halving(candidates, evaluate, budget0=1, eta=2):
    """Budgeted search: evaluate everyone cheaply, keep the best 1/eta, give survivors eta x more budget.

    Round r: |pool| = N / eta**r candidates, each evaluated with budget0 * eta**r.
    Bad candidates are dropped early on cheap (noisy) evaluations; the
    expensive, accurate evaluations are spent only on the few finalists.

    Args:
        candidates: iterable of candidates.
        evaluate:   evaluate(candidate, budget) -> score (higher is better).
                    Larger budget should mean less noisy score.
        budget0:    budget per candidate in the first round.
        eta:        keep the top 1/eta each round and multiply the budget by eta.
    Returns:
        the single surviving candidate.
    """
    pool = list(candidates)
    budget = budget0

    while len(pool) > 1:
        # Step 1: score every remaining candidate at the current budget
        # (each candidate is evaluated exactly once per round).
        ranked = sorted(pool, key=lambda candidate: evaluate(candidate, budget), reverse=True)

        # Step 2: keep the top 1/eta (at least one survivor).
        num_survivors = max(1, len(pool) // eta)
        pool = ranked[:num_survivors]

        # Step 3: survivors get a bigger budget next round.
        budget = budget * eta

    return pool[0]


if __name__ == "__main__":
    random.seed(0)

    # --- 1. worker pool: flaky jobs recover via retries; item 13 always times out ---
    async def flaky(x):
        """Fails transiently 30% of the time; item 13 hangs forever."""
        await asyncio.sleep(random.random() * 0.02)
        if random.random() < 0.3:
            raise RuntimeError("transient")
        if x == 13:
            await asyncio.sleep(10)           # always times out
        return x * x

    results = asyncio.run(worker_pool(flaky, range(20), concurrency=5, timeout=0.2, retries=3))
    succeeded = [r for r in results if r["ok"]]
    failed_items = [r["item"] for r in results if not r["ok"]]
    print(f"worker pool: {len(succeeded)}/20 ok, failed: {failed_items}")
    assert all(r["ok"] for r in results if r["item"] != 13) and not results[13]["ok"]

    # --- 2. UCB1: should pull the best arm (index 2, p=0.8) most often ---
    arm_probs = [0.2, 0.5, 0.8, 0.4]
    bandit = UCB1(4)
    for _ in range(3000):
        arm = bandit.select()
        reward = float(random.random() < arm_probs[arm])
        bandit.update(arm, reward)
    print("UCB pulls", bandit.n)
    assert max(range(4), key=lambda i: bandit.n[i]) == 2

    # --- 3. paired bootstrap: B is A + 0.03 on average -> CI should exclude 0 ---
    scores_a = [random.gauss(0.5, 0.1) for _ in range(100)]
    scores_b = [x + 0.03 + random.gauss(0, 0.05) for x in scores_a]
    diff, ci, p = paired_bootstrap(scores_a, scores_b)
    print(f"bootstrap diff {diff:.3f} CI ({ci[0]:.3f},{ci[1]:.3f}) p={p:.4f}")
    assert ci[0] > 0

    # --- 4. cache: three identical requests -> only one real run ---
    cache = ResultCache()
    run_calls = []

    def expensive_run():
        """Pretend experiment that records each time it is actually executed."""
        run_calls.append(1)
        return 0.9

    for _ in range(3):
        cache.get_or_run("print(1)", {"lr": 1e-3}, 0, expensive_run)
    assert len(run_calls) == 1
    print("cache OK (1 run for 3 requests)")

    # --- 5. successive halving: noise shrinks as 1/sqrt(budget) ---
    true_quality = {f"c{i}": random.random() for i in range(16)}

    def noisy_evaluate(candidate, budget):
        """True quality plus Gaussian noise whose std shrinks with budget."""
        return true_quality[candidate] + random.gauss(0, 0.3 / math.sqrt(budget))

    best = successive_halving(true_quality, noisy_evaluate)
    print("successive halving picked", best, "true best", max(true_quality, key=true_quality.get))
    print("OK")
10_leetcode_patterns.py — LeetCode patterns: 16 patterns, ~35 canonical problems with tests download · 
"""LeetCode-style patterns: one canonical problem per pattern, with tests.

Pattern -> problem (LC number)
 1. Hashing            two_sum (1), group_anagrams (49), subarray_sum_k (560, prefix sum + hashmap)
 2. Sliding window     longest_unique_substring (3), min_window (76)
 3. Two pointers       three_sum (15), max_area (11)
 4. Binary search      search_rotated (33), min_eating_speed (875, binary search on answer)
 5. Heap               top_k_frequent (347), merge_k_sorted (23), MedianFinder (295)
 6. Monotonic stack    daily_temperatures (739), largest_rectangle (84)
 7. Intervals          merge_intervals (56), min_meeting_rooms (253)
 8. Graph BFS/DFS      num_islands (200), shortest_path_grid (1091)
 9. Topological sort   can_finish / course_order (207/210)
10. Union-Find         count_components (323)
11. Dijkstra           network_delay (743)
12. Trie               Trie (208)
13. Backtracking       subsets (78), permutations (46), combination_sum (39)
14. DP                 coin_change (322), lis (300, O(n log n)), edit_distance (72), lcs (1143)
15. Design             LRUCache (146, dict + doubly linked list)
16. ML-flavoured       reservoir_sample, weighted_sample (528), sparse_matmul, kth_largest (215, quickselect)

Interview checklist:
- state brute force + complexity first, then optimise; say time AND space complexity at the end
- walk through a tiny example by hand before coding; test edge cases: empty, single element, duplicates, negatives
"""
import bisect
import heapq
import random
from collections import Counter, OrderedDict, defaultdict, deque


# =====================================================================
# 1. Hashing
# =====================================================================
def two_sum(nums, target):
    """LC 1. Return indices [i, j] such that nums[i] + nums[j] == target.

    Idea: while scanning, remember every value we've already seen and its index.
    For the current number, the partner we need is (target - current). If that
    partner is already in the hashmap, we're done.

    Time O(n), space O(n).
    """
    index_of_value = {}  # value -> index where we saw it

    for current_index, current_value in enumerate(nums):
        needed_partner = target - current_value

        # Did we already see a number that completes the pair?
        if needed_partner in index_of_value:
            partner_index = index_of_value[needed_partner]
            return [partner_index, current_index]

        # Store AFTER checking, so we never pair an element with itself.
        index_of_value[current_value] = current_index

    return []  # no valid pair


def group_anagrams(words):
    """LC 49. Group words that are anagrams of each other.

    Idea: two words are anagrams iff they have the same letter counts.
    Use the 26-length letter-count tuple as a hashmap key.
    (Alternative key: ''.join(sorted(word)), which costs O(k log k) per word.)

    Time O(n * k) where k = average word length, space O(n * k).
    """
    groups = defaultdict(list)  # letter-count signature -> list of words

    for word in words:
        letter_counts = [0] * 26
        for char in word:
            letter_index = ord(char) - ord("a")
            letter_counts[letter_index] += 1

        # Lists are not hashable, so convert to a tuple to use as a dict key.
        signature = tuple(letter_counts)
        groups[signature].append(word)

    return list(groups.values())


def subarray_sum_k(nums, k):
    """LC 560. Count contiguous subarrays whose sum equals k.

    Idea (prefix sum + hashmap):
        sum(nums[i+1..j]) = prefix[j] - prefix[i]
    So a subarray ending at j sums to k  <=>  some earlier prefix == prefix[j] - k.
    Keep a count of how many times each prefix sum has appeared so far.
    Works with negative numbers (sliding window would not).

    Time O(n), space O(n).
    """
    # The empty prefix (before any element) has sum 0 and appears once.
    # This lets subarrays that start at index 0 be counted.
    prefix_sum_count = Counter()
    prefix_sum_count[0] = 1

    running_sum = 0
    subarray_count = 0

    for value in nums:
        running_sum += value

        # If (running_sum - k) was seen before, each occurrence marks a start
        # point where the subarray ending here sums to exactly k.
        needed_prefix = running_sum - k
        subarray_count += prefix_sum_count[needed_prefix]

        # Record the current prefix so later positions can use it.
        prefix_sum_count[running_sum] += 1

    return subarray_count


# =====================================================================
# 2. Sliding window
# =====================================================================
def longest_unique_substring(s):
    """LC 3. Length of the longest substring without repeating characters.

    Idea: sliding window [left, right]. Invariant: the window contains only
    unique characters. When s[right] already appears inside the window, jump
    `left` to just after its previous position.

    Time O(n), space O(min(n, alphabet size)).
    """
    last_seen_index = {}  # char -> most recent index where it appeared
    left = 0
    best_length = 0

    for right, char in enumerate(s):
        # Only a duplicate if its previous occurrence is INSIDE the current window.
        if char in last_seen_index and last_seen_index[char] >= left:
            left = last_seen_index[char] + 1  # shrink window past the duplicate

        last_seen_index[char] = right

        window_length = right - left + 1
        best_length = max(best_length, window_length)

    return best_length


def min_window(s, t):
    """LC 76. Smallest substring of s that contains every char of t (with multiplicity).

    Idea: sliding window.
      1. Expand `right` until the window covers all of t (chars_missing == 0).
      2. Then shrink `left` as much as possible while it still covers t,
         recording the best (shortest) window along the way.
    need[c] = how many more copies of c the window still needs (can go negative
    when the window has extras).

    Time O(|s| + |t|), space O(|t|).
    """
    need = Counter(t)
    chars_missing = len(t)  # total chars of t not yet covered by the window

    left = 0
    best_start = 0
    best_length = float("inf")

    for right, char in enumerate(s):
        # --- expand: add s[right] to the window ---
        if need[char] > 0:
            chars_missing -= 1  # this char was actually useful
        need[char] -= 1

        # --- shrink: window is valid, try to make it smaller ---
        while chars_missing == 0:
            window_length = right - left + 1
            if window_length < best_length:
                best_start = left
                best_length = window_length

            # Remove s[left] from the window.
            left_char = s[left]
            need[left_char] += 1
            if need[left_char] > 0:
                chars_missing += 1  # we just dropped a required char -> window invalid
            left += 1

    if best_length == float("inf"):
        return ""
    return s[best_start:best_start + best_length]


# =====================================================================
# 3. Two pointers
# =====================================================================
def three_sum(nums):
    """LC 15. All unique triplets [a, b, c] with a + b + c == 0.

    Idea: sort, fix the first element nums[i], then find pairs in the rest with
    two pointers (left from the start, right from the end). Skip duplicate
    values to avoid repeated triplets.

    Time O(n^2), space O(1) extra (ignoring output and the sort).
    """
    sorted_nums = sorted(nums)
    n = len(sorted_nums)
    triplets = []

    for i in range(n - 2):
        # Skip duplicate first elements: same value would give the same triplets.
        if i > 0 and sorted_nums[i] == sorted_nums[i - 1]:
            continue

        left = i + 1
        right = n - 1

        while left < right:
            total = sorted_nums[i] + sorted_nums[left] + sorted_nums[right]

            if total < 0:
                left += 1  # need a bigger sum -> move left pointer right
            elif total > 0:
                right -= 1  # need a smaller sum -> move right pointer left
            else:
                triplets.append([sorted_nums[i], sorted_nums[left], sorted_nums[right]])

                # Skip duplicate second elements.
                while left < right and sorted_nums[left] == sorted_nums[left + 1]:
                    left += 1

                left += 1
                right -= 1

    return triplets


def max_area(h):
    """LC 11. Container with most water: pick two lines maximising width * min height.

    Idea: start with the widest container (both ends). The area is limited by
    the SHORTER line, so moving the taller one inward can never help — always
    move the shorter side.

    Time O(n), space O(1).
    """
    heights = h
    left = 0
    right = len(heights) - 1
    best_area = 0

    while left < right:
        width = right - left
        water_height = min(heights[left], heights[right])
        area = width * water_height
        best_area = max(best_area, area)

        # Move the shorter wall: it's the bottleneck.
        if heights[left] < heights[right]:
            left += 1
        else:
            right -= 1

    return best_area


# =====================================================================
# 4. Binary search
# =====================================================================
def search_rotated(nums, target):
    """LC 33. Find target's index in a rotated sorted array (distinct values), or -1.

    Idea: at any mid, at least one half [lo..mid] or [mid..hi] is sorted.
    Check whether target lies inside the sorted half; if yes search there,
    otherwise search the other half.

    Time O(log n), space O(1).
    """
    lo = 0
    hi = len(nums) - 1

    while lo <= hi:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2

        if nums[mid] == target:
            return mid

        left_half_is_sorted = nums[lo] <= nums[mid]

        if left_half_is_sorted:
            target_in_left_half = nums[lo] <= target < nums[mid]
            if target_in_left_half:
                hi = mid - 1
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        else:
            # Then the right half [mid..hi] must be sorted.
            target_in_right_half = nums[mid] < target <= nums[hi]
            if target_in_right_half:
                lo = mid + 1
            else:
                hi = mid - 1

    return -1


def min_eating_speed(piles, h):
    """LC 875. Koko eats bananas: smallest speed k so all piles finish within h hours.

    Idea: "binary search on the answer". If speed k works, any speed > k also
    works (monotonic). So binary search the smallest feasible k in [1, max(piles)].

    Time O(n log max(piles)), space O(1).
    """

    def hours_needed(speed):
        total_hours = 0
        for pile in piles:
            # ceil(pile / speed) without floats
            total_hours += (pile + speed - 1) // speed
        return total_hours

    lo = 1
    hi = max(piles)  # at this speed every pile takes exactly 1 hour

    # Invariant: the answer is always within [lo, hi].
    while lo < hi:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if hours_needed(mid) <= h:
            hi = mid  # mid works; maybe something smaller works too
        else:
            lo = mid + 1  # mid too slow; answer must be bigger

    return lo


# =====================================================================
# 5. Heap
# =====================================================================
def top_k_frequent(nums, k):
    """LC 347. Return the k most frequent elements.

    Idea: count with a hashmap, then keep a MIN-heap of size k keyed by
    frequency. Whenever the heap grows beyond k, pop the least frequent.
    What's left are the k most frequent.
    (Alternative: bucket sort by frequency for O(n).)

    Time O(n log k), space O(n).
    """
    frequency = Counter(nums)

    min_heap = []  # (count, value); smallest count on top
    for value, count in frequency.items():
        heapq.heappush(min_heap, (count, value))
        if len(min_heap) > k:
            heapq.heappop(min_heap)  # drop the least frequent so far

    result = []
    for count, value in min_heap:
        result.append(value)
    return result


def merge_k_sorted(lists):
    """LC 23. Merge k sorted lists into one sorted list.

    Idea: a min-heap holds the current front element of each list. Pop the
    smallest, append it to the output, and push the next element from the
    same list.

    Time O(N log k) where N = total elements, space O(k) for the heap.
    """
    # Heap entries: (value, which_list, index_in_that_list).
    # which_list also breaks ties so we never compare beyond integers.
    min_heap = []
    for list_index, current_list in enumerate(lists):
        if current_list:  # skip empty lists
            min_heap.append((current_list[0], list_index, 0))
    heapq.heapify(min_heap)

    merged = []
    while min_heap:
        value, list_index, element_index = heapq.heappop(min_heap)
        merged.append(value)

        next_index = element_index + 1
        source_list = lists[list_index]
        if next_index < len(source_list):
            heapq.heappush(min_heap, (source_list[next_index], list_index, next_index))

    return merged


class MedianFinder:
    """LC 295. Stream median.

    Idea: two heaps.
      - small_half: MAX-heap with the smaller half (stored negated, since
        heapq is a min-heap).
      - large_half: MIN-heap with the larger half.
    Invariants: every element in small_half <= every element in large_half,
    and len(small_half) == len(large_half) or len(large_half) + 1.

    add: O(log n), median: O(1).
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self.small_half = []  # max-heap via negation
        self.large_half = []  # min-heap

    def add(self, x):
        # Step 1: push into small half.
        heapq.heappush(self.small_half, -x)

        # Step 2: move small half's largest into large half, which keeps the
        # ordering invariant (everything in small <= everything in large).
        largest_of_small = -heapq.heappop(self.small_half)
        heapq.heappush(self.large_half, largest_of_small)

        # Step 3: rebalance sizes so small half is never smaller than large half.
        if len(self.large_half) > len(self.small_half):
            smallest_of_large = heapq.heappop(self.large_half)
            heapq.heappush(self.small_half, -smallest_of_large)

    def median(self):
        max_of_small = -self.small_half[0]

        # Odd count: small half holds the extra element, which is the median.
        if len(self.small_half) > len(self.large_half):
            return max_of_small

        # Even count: average of the two middle elements.
        min_of_large = self.large_half[0]
        return (max_of_small + min_of_large) / 2


# =====================================================================
# 6. Monotonic stack
# =====================================================================
def daily_temperatures(t):
    """LC 739. For each day, how many days until a warmer temperature (0 if never).

    Idea: monotonic DECREASING stack of indices still waiting for a warmer day.
    When today is warmer than the day on top of the stack, that day's answer
    is found: pop it and record the distance.

    Time O(n) (each index pushed/popped once), space O(n).
    """
    temperatures = t
    days_to_wait = [0] * len(temperatures)
    waiting_stack = []  # indices; their temperatures are decreasing bottom -> top

    for today, today_temp in enumerate(temperatures):
        # Today resolves every colder day on top of the stack.
        while waiting_stack and temperatures[waiting_stack[-1]] < today_temp:
            colder_day = waiting_stack.pop()
            days_to_wait[colder_day] = today - colder_day

        waiting_stack.append(today)

    # Anything left in the stack never sees a warmer day -> stays 0.
    return days_to_wait


def largest_rectangle(heights):
    """LC 84. Largest rectangle area in a histogram.

    Idea: monotonic INCREASING stack of (start_index, height). When a shorter
    bar arrives, every taller bar on the stack can't extend further right, so
    pop it and compute its area. The new bar can extend LEFT to where the
    popped bar started.
    A sentinel height 0 at the end flushes the stack.

    Time O(n), space O(n).
    """
    stack = []  # (start_index, height); heights increasing bottom -> top
    best_area = 0
    heights_with_sentinel = heights + [0]

    for index, height in enumerate(heights_with_sentinel):
        start_index = index

        # Pop taller (or equal) bars: their rectangle ends right before `index`.
        while stack and stack[-1][1] >= height:
            popped_start, popped_height = stack.pop()
            width = index - popped_start
            best_area = max(best_area, popped_height * width)

            # The current, shorter bar can stretch back to where the popped one began.
            start_index = popped_start

        stack.append((start_index, height))

    return best_area


# =====================================================================
# 7. Intervals
# =====================================================================
def merge_intervals(iv):
    """LC 56. Merge all overlapping intervals.

    Idea: sort by start. Then an interval overlaps the previous merged one iff
    its start <= the last merged end. If so, extend that end; otherwise start a
    new merged interval.

    Time O(n log n), space O(n).
    """
    intervals = sorted(iv)  # sorts by start, then end
    merged = []

    for start, end in intervals:
        if merged and start <= merged[-1][1]:
            # Overlaps the last merged interval -> extend it.
            last_end = merged[-1][1]
            merged[-1][1] = max(last_end, end)
        else:
            # No overlap -> new interval.
            merged.append([start, end])

    return merged


def min_meeting_rooms(iv):
    """LC 253. Minimum number of meeting rooms needed.

    Idea: process meetings by start time. Keep a min-heap of END times of
    meetings currently using a room. If the earliest-ending room is free by
    the time this meeting starts, reuse it; otherwise open a new room.
    The heap size at the end = rooms needed.

    Time O(n log n), space O(n).
    """
    meetings = sorted(iv)
    room_end_times = []  # min-heap: when each occupied room frees up

    for start, end in meetings:
        earliest_free_room_available = room_end_times and room_end_times[0] <= start

        if earliest_free_room_available:
            # Reuse that room: replace its end time with this meeting's end.
            heapq.heapreplace(room_end_times, end)
        else:
            # All rooms busy: need a new one.
            heapq.heappush(room_end_times, end)

    return len(room_end_times)


# =====================================================================
# 8. Graph BFS / DFS
# =====================================================================
def num_islands(grid):
    """LC 200. Count islands of '1's (4-directionally connected) in a grid.

    Idea: scan every cell. When we find unvisited land, that's a new island:
    flood-fill (DFS) to mark all connected land as visited ("0").
    Iterative stack to avoid Python's recursion limit on big grids.

    Time O(R * C), space O(R * C).
    """
    # Copy so we don't mutate the caller's grid.
    grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
    if not grid:
        return 0

    num_rows = len(grid)
    num_cols = len(grid[0])
    island_count = 0
    directions = [(1, 0), (-1, 0), (0, 1), (0, -1)]

    for row in range(num_rows):
        for col in range(num_cols):
            if grid[row][col] != "1":
                continue

            # Found a new island -> sink all of it.
            island_count += 1
            grid[row][col] = "0"  # mark visited when pushing
            stack = [(row, col)]

            while stack:
                cur_row, cur_col = stack.pop()
                for d_row, d_col in directions:
                    next_row = cur_row + d_row
                    next_col = cur_col + d_col
                    inside_grid = 0 <= next_row < num_rows and 0 <= next_col < num_cols
                    if inside_grid and grid[next_row][next_col] == "1":
                        grid[next_row][next_col] = "0"
                        stack.append((next_row, next_col))

    return island_count


def shortest_path_grid(grid):
    """LC 1091. Shortest clear path (0 = free, 1 = blocked) from top-left to
    bottom-right in an n x n grid, moving in 8 directions. Path length counts cells.

    Idea: BFS from the start; BFS explores in order of distance, so the first
    time we reach the target is the shortest path.

    Time O(n^2), space O(n^2).
    """
    n = len(grid)
    start_blocked = grid[0][0] == 1
    end_blocked = grid[n - 1][n - 1] == 1
    if start_blocked or end_blocked:
        return -1

    target = (n - 1, n - 1)
    queue = deque()
    queue.append((0, 0, 1))  # (row, col, path_length_so_far)
    visited = {(0, 0)}

    while queue:
        row, col, path_length = queue.popleft()

        if (row, col) == target:
            return path_length

        # All 8 neighbours (d_row, d_col) in {-1,0,1}^2 except (0,0).
        for d_row in (-1, 0, 1):
            for d_col in (-1, 0, 1):
                if d_row == 0 and d_col == 0:
                    continue
                next_row = row + d_row
                next_col = col + d_col

                if not (0 <= next_row < n and 0 <= next_col < n):
                    continue
                if grid[next_row][next_col] == 1:
                    continue
                if (next_row, next_col) in visited:
                    continue

                # Mark visited when ENQUEUING, so each cell is queued at most once.
                visited.add((next_row, next_col))
                queue.append((next_row, next_col, path_length + 1))

    return -1


# =====================================================================
# 9. Topological sort
# =====================================================================
def course_order(n, prereqs):
    """LC 210. Return an order to take n courses given [course, prerequisite]
    pairs; return [] if impossible (there is a cycle).

    Idea: Kahn's algorithm (BFS topological sort). Repeatedly take a course
    with no remaining prerequisites (in-degree 0), then "remove" it, lowering
    the in-degree of courses that depend on it.
    If some courses are never taken, they're stuck in a cycle.

    Time O(V + E), space O(V + E).
    """
    dependents = defaultdict(list)  # prerequisite -> courses that need it
    in_degree = [0] * n  # number of unmet prerequisites per course

    for course, prerequisite in prereqs:
        # Edge: prerequisite -> course
        dependents[prerequisite].append(course)
        in_degree[course] += 1

    # Start with courses that have no prerequisites.
    ready_queue = deque()
    for course in range(n):
        if in_degree[course] == 0:
            ready_queue.append(course)

    order = []
    while ready_queue:
        course = ready_queue.popleft()
        order.append(course)

        # Taking this course satisfies one prerequisite for each dependent.
        for next_course in dependents[course]:
            in_degree[next_course] -= 1
            if in_degree[next_course] == 0:
                ready_queue.append(next_course)

    all_courses_taken = len(order) == n
    if all_courses_taken:
        return order
    return []  # cycle detected


def can_finish(n, prereqs):
    """LC 207. Can all courses be finished? True iff the prerequisite graph has
    no cycle, i.e. a full topological order exists. Time O(V + E).
    """
    order = course_order(n, prereqs)
    return len(order) == n


# =====================================================================
# 10. Union-Find
# =====================================================================
class DSU:
    """Disjoint Set Union (Union-Find) with path compression + union by rank.

    find / union are amortised ~O(1) (inverse Ackermann).
    """

    def __init__(self, n):
        self.parent = list(range(n))  # initially every node is its own root
        self.rank = [0] * n  # upper bound on tree height

    def find(self, x):
        """Return the root of x's set."""
        while self.parent[x] != x:
            # Path halving: point x to its grandparent, flattening the tree.
            self.parent[x] = self.parent[self.parent[x]]
            x = self.parent[x]
        return x

    def union(self, a, b):
        """Merge the sets of a and b. Return True if they were separate."""
        root_a = self.find(a)
        root_b = self.find(b)

        if root_a == root_b:
            return False  # already in the same set

        # Attach the shorter tree under the taller one.
        if self.rank[root_a] < self.rank[root_b]:
            root_a, root_b = root_b, root_a
        self.parent[root_b] = root_a

        # Height only grows when both trees had equal rank.
        if self.rank[root_a] == self.rank[root_b]:
            self.rank[root_a] += 1

        return True


def count_components(n, edges):
    """LC 323. Number of connected components in an undirected graph.

    Idea: start with n components; every edge that joins two DIFFERENT
    components reduces the count by one.

    Time O(E * alpha(n)), space O(n).
    """
    dsu = DSU(n)
    components = n

    for a, b in edges:
        merged_two_components = dsu.union(a, b)
        if merged_two_components:
            components -= 1

    return components


# =====================================================================
# 11. Dijkstra
# =====================================================================
def network_delay(times, n, k):
    """LC 743. Signal from node k: time until all n nodes receive it, or -1.
    times = [u, v, w] directed edges with travel time w.

    Idea: Dijkstra with a min-heap. The first time a node is popped, its
    distance is final (all weights non-negative). Answer = the largest
    shortest distance.

    Time O(E log V), space O(V + E).
    """
    graph = defaultdict(list)  # node -> [(neighbour, weight)]
    for source, destination, weight in times:
        graph[source].append((destination, weight))

    shortest_distance = {}  # finalised distances
    min_heap = [(0, k)]  # (distance from k, node)

    while min_heap:
        distance, node = heapq.heappop(min_heap)

        # A node can be pushed several times; only the first pop matters.
        if node in shortest_distance:
            continue
        shortest_distance[node] = distance

        for neighbour, weight in graph[node]:
            if neighbour not in shortest_distance:
                heapq.heappush(min_heap, (distance + weight, neighbour))

    every_node_reached = len(shortest_distance) == n
    if not every_node_reached:
        return -1
    return max(shortest_distance.values())


# =====================================================================
# 12. Trie
# =====================================================================
class Trie:
    """LC 208. Prefix tree supporting insert / search / starts_with.

    Each node is a dict: char -> child node. The special key "$" marks that a
    complete word ends at this node.

    All operations O(L) where L = word length.
    """

    END_OF_WORD = "$"

    def __init__(self):
        self.root = {}

    def insert(self, w):
        node = self.root
        for char in w:
            if char not in node:
                node[char] = {}  # create the missing child
            node = node[char]
        node[self.END_OF_WORD] = True

    def _walk(self, w):
        """Follow w's characters from the root; return the final node or None."""
        node = self.root
        for char in w:
            if char not in node:
                return None
            node = node[char]
        return node

    def search(self, w):
        """True only if w was inserted as a whole word (not just a prefix)."""
        node = self._walk(w)
        if node is None:
            return False
        return node.get(self.END_OF_WORD, False)

    def starts_with(self, p):
        """True if any inserted word starts with prefix p."""
        node = self._walk(p)
        return node is not None


# =====================================================================
# 13. Backtracking
# =====================================================================
def subsets(nums):
    """LC 78. All subsets (power set) of distinct nums.

    Idea: for each index, make a binary choice — skip nums[i] or take it.
    Recurse until we've decided for every element.

    Time O(n * 2^n), space O(n) recursion depth (plus output).
    """
    all_subsets = []
    current_subset = []

    def backtrack(index):
        # Decided for every element -> record a copy of the current subset.
        if index == len(nums):
            all_subsets.append(current_subset[:])
            return

        # Choice 1: skip nums[index].
        backtrack(index + 1)

        # Choice 2: take nums[index], recurse, then undo the choice.
        current_subset.append(nums[index])
        backtrack(index + 1)
        current_subset.pop()

    backtrack(0)
    return all_subsets


def permutations(nums):
    """LC 46. All permutations of distinct nums.

    Idea: build the permutation one position at a time; at each step try every
    element not yet used. `used[i]` tracks which elements are already placed.

    Time O(n * n!), space O(n) recursion depth (plus output).
    """
    all_permutations = []
    current_permutation = []
    used = [False] * len(nums)

    def backtrack():
        if len(current_permutation) == len(nums):
            all_permutations.append(current_permutation[:])
            return

        for i in range(len(nums)):
            if used[i]:
                continue

            # Choose
            used[i] = True
            current_permutation.append(nums[i])

            # Explore
            backtrack()

            # Un-choose (restore state for the next candidate)
            current_permutation.pop()
            used[i] = False

    backtrack()
    return all_permutations


def combination_sum(cands, target):
    """LC 39. All unique combinations of candidates summing to target; each
    candidate may be reused any number of times.

    Idea: backtracking. To avoid duplicates like [2,3] and [3,2], only pick
    candidates at index >= start (non-decreasing order). Passing `i` (not i+1)
    allows reuse. Sorting lets us stop early once a candidate exceeds what's left.

    Time exponential (roughly O(N^(T/min))), space O(T/min) recursion depth.
    """
    candidates = sorted(cands)
    all_combinations = []
    current_combination = []

    def backtrack(start_index, remaining):
        if remaining == 0:
            all_combinations.append(current_combination[:])
            return

        for i in range(start_index, len(candidates)):
            candidate = candidates[i]

            # Sorted: if this one is too big, all later ones are too.
            if candidate > remaining:
                break

            current_combination.append(candidate)
            backtrack(i, remaining - candidate)  # i, not i+1: reuse allowed
            current_combination.pop()

    backtrack(0, target)
    return all_combinations


# =====================================================================
# 14. Dynamic programming
# =====================================================================
def coin_change(coins, amount):
    """LC 322. Fewest coins to make `amount` (unlimited coins); -1 if impossible.

    DP: min_coins[a] = fewest coins summing to a.
        min_coins[0] = 0
        min_coins[a] = 1 + min(min_coins[a - coin]) over coins <= a

    Time O(amount * len(coins)), space O(amount).
    """
    IMPOSSIBLE = amount + 1  # more coins than could ever be needed (acts as infinity)

    min_coins = [IMPOSSIBLE] * (amount + 1)
    min_coins[0] = 0

    for current_amount in range(1, amount + 1):
        for coin in coins:
            if coin > current_amount:
                continue
            # Use this coin last: 1 coin + best way to make the rest.
            candidate = min_coins[current_amount - coin] + 1
            if candidate < min_coins[current_amount]:
                min_coins[current_amount] = candidate

    if min_coins[amount] == IMPOSSIBLE:
        return -1
    return min_coins[amount]


def lis(nums):
    """LC 300. Length of the longest strictly increasing subsequence.

    Idea (patience sorting): tails[i] = smallest possible tail value of an
    increasing subsequence of length i+1. `tails` stays sorted, so for each
    number we binary-search where it goes:
      - larger than every tail -> extends the longest subsequence (append)
      - otherwise -> replace the first tail >= it (a smaller tail is better)
    len(tails) is the answer (tails itself is NOT necessarily a valid LIS).

    Time O(n log n), space O(n).
    """
    tails = []

    for number in nums:
        position = bisect.bisect_left(tails, number)  # first tail >= number

        if position == len(tails):
            tails.append(number)
        else:
            tails[position] = number

    return len(tails)


def edit_distance(a, b):
    """LC 72. Minimum insert/delete/replace operations to turn a into b.

    DP: dist[i][j] = edit distance between a[:i] and b[:j].
      - if a[i-1] == b[j-1]: dist[i][j] = dist[i-1][j-1]          (no cost)
      - else: 1 + min(dist[i-1][j-1],   # replace
                      dist[i-1][j],     # delete from a
                      dist[i][j-1])     # insert into a
    Only the previous row is needed, so keep two rows.

    Time O(m * n), space O(n).
    """
    len_a = len(a)
    len_b = len(b)

    # Row for i = 0: turning "" into b[:j] takes j insertions.
    previous_row = list(range(len_b + 1))

    for i in range(1, len_a + 1):
        current_row = [0] * (len_b + 1)
        current_row[0] = i  # turning a[:i] into "" takes i deletions

        for j in range(1, len_b + 1):
            if a[i - 1] == b[j - 1]:
                current_row[j] = previous_row[j - 1]
            else:
                replace_cost = previous_row[j - 1]
                delete_cost = previous_row[j]
                insert_cost = current_row[j - 1]
                current_row[j] = 1 + min(replace_cost, delete_cost, insert_cost)

        previous_row = current_row

    return previous_row[len_b]


def lcs(a, b):
    """LC 1143. Length of the longest common subsequence of a and b.

    DP: common[i][j] = LCS length of a[:i] and b[:j].
      - if a[i-1] == b[j-1]: common[i][j] = common[i-1][j-1] + 1
      - else:                common[i][j] = max(common[i-1][j], common[i][j-1])

    Time O(m * n), space O(m * n).
    """
    len_a = len(a)
    len_b = len(b)

    # Extra row/column of zeros for empty prefixes.
    common = []
    for _ in range(len_a + 1):
        common.append([0] * (len_b + 1))

    for i in range(1, len_a + 1):
        for j in range(1, len_b + 1):
            if a[i - 1] == b[j - 1]:
                # Matching chars extend the LCS of both shorter prefixes.
                common[i][j] = common[i - 1][j - 1] + 1
            else:
                # Drop the last char of a or of b, whichever is better.
                common[i][j] = max(common[i - 1][j], common[i][j - 1])

    return common[len_a][len_b]


# =====================================================================
# 15. Design: LRU cache
# =====================================================================
class _Node:
    """Doubly linked list node used by LRUCache."""

    __slots__ = ("k", "v", "prev", "next")

    def __init__(self, k=None, v=None):
        self.k = k  # key (needed to delete from the hashmap on eviction)
        self.v = v  # value
        self.prev = None
        self.next = None


class LRUCache:
    """LC 146. Least-Recently-Used cache with O(1) get and put.

    Idea: hashmap (key -> node) for O(1) lookup + doubly linked list for O(1)
    reordering. List order = recency: right after `head` is the most recently
    used, right before `tail` is the least recently used.
    `head` and `tail` are dummy sentinels so we never special-case empty lists.
    (In Python, collections.OrderedDict + move_to_end also works.)
    """

    def __init__(self, cap):
        self.capacity = cap
        self.node_of_key = {}

        self.head = _Node()  # sentinel: head.next = most recently used
        self.tail = _Node()  # sentinel: tail.prev = least recently used
        self.head.next = self.tail
        self.tail.prev = self.head

    def _remove(self, node):
        """Unlink node from the list."""
        previous_node = node.prev
        next_node = node.next
        previous_node.next = next_node
        next_node.prev = previous_node

    def _add_front(self, node):
        """Insert node right after head (= mark as most recently used)."""
        first_real_node = self.head.next

        node.prev = self.head
        node.next = first_real_node

        first_real_node.prev = node
        self.head.next = node

    def get(self, k):
        if k not in self.node_of_key:
            return -1

        node = self.node_of_key[k]

        # Accessing a key makes it the most recently used.
        self._remove(node)
        self._add_front(node)

        return node.v

    def put(self, k, v):
        # If the key exists, drop the old node; we'll insert a fresh one.
        if k in self.node_of_key:
            old_node = self.node_of_key[k]
            self._remove(old_node)

        new_node = _Node(k, v)
        self.node_of_key[k] = new_node
        self._add_front(new_node)

        # Over capacity -> evict the least recently used (just before tail).
        if len(self.node_of_key) > self.capacity:
            least_recent = self.tail.prev
            self._remove(least_recent)
            del self.node_of_key[least_recent.k]


# =====================================================================
# 16. ML-flavoured
# =====================================================================
def reservoir_sample(stream, k, rng=random):
    """Pick k items uniformly at random from a stream of unknown length,
    using O(k) memory (one pass).

    Idea (Algorithm R): keep the first k items. For the i-th item (0-indexed,
    i >= k), keep it with probability k / (i + 1) by drawing j in [0, i]; if
    j < k, it replaces reservoir[j]. By induction every item ends up in the
    reservoir with probability k / n.

    Time O(n), space O(k).
    """
    reservoir = []

    for index, item in enumerate(stream):
        if index < k:
            # Fill the reservoir first.
            reservoir.append(item)
            continue

        random_slot = rng.randint(0, index)  # inclusive on both ends
        if random_slot < k:
            # Happens with probability k / (index + 1).
            reservoir[random_slot] = item

    return reservoir


class WeightedSampler:
    """LC 528. Random pick with weight: return index i with probability
    w[i] / sum(w).

    Idea: build prefix sums; this splits [0, total) into segments whose lengths
    are the weights. Draw a uniform number in [0, total) and binary-search which
    segment it lands in.

    __init__ O(n), pick O(log n).
    """

    def __init__(self, w):
        self.cum = []  # cum[i] = w[0] + ... + w[i]
        running_total = 0
        for weight in w:
            running_total += weight
            self.cum.append(running_total)

    def pick(self, rng=random):
        total_weight = self.cum[-1]
        point = rng.random() * total_weight  # uniform in [0, total_weight)

        # Index i such that cum[i-1] <= point < cum[i].
        return bisect.bisect_right(self.cum, point)


def sparse_matmul(A, B):
    """LC 311. Multiply matrices A (m x k) and B (k x n) that are mostly zeros.

    Idea: only non-zero entries contribute. Pre-store each row of B as a dict
    {column: value} of non-zeros, then for every non-zero A[i][k] add
    A[i][k] * B[k][j] to out[i][j] for the non-zero B[k][j].

    Time O(nnz(A) * avg nnz per B row), space O(nnz(B) + m * n).
    """
    num_rows_out = len(A)
